# Dashboard Scanner — Version History (CHANGELOG)

Relocated out of scanner.py (v1.346.0, owner-approved) — the full history previously lived as one
~290 KB single-line comment on the SCAN_VERSION line, where any unanchored grep could dump it whole
into a session (measured: one such dump consumed 28% of an entire working session). This file is the
authoritative history; scanner.py now carries only the current version with a one-line note.

CONVENTION from v1.346.0 on: each new scanner version adds its full rationale as a new entry at the
TOP of this file, and scanner.py keeps a one-line summary only.

---

## Reconstruction note (2026-08-14)

Entries v1.347.0 - v1.430.0 below were appended in one owner-approved sweep on 2026-08-14: the per-version relocation convention had lapsed after v1.346.0, and each version's full docstring was overwritten in scanner.py by the next bump. v1.418-430 are taken from the audited Dashboard_Changelog_2026-08-14; v1.387-417 from the audited Dashboard_Changelog_2026-08-10 (v1.387-396 grouped, as documented there); v1.347-386 are reconstructed verbatim-where-possible from the archived session transcripts' delivered docstrings and may be truncated relative to the lost originals. Convention resumes: every new version adds its full rationale here at the top.

## index v5.345 (display; scanner v1.442.0 already live)

FORWARD-OUTLOOK COT LINE now names COMMERCIALS. Audit of the deployed v5.344 found the commercial/hedger split + plain language DID render -- but only in the 'Trader Positioning x Seasonality' strip; the per-metal COT line inside the Forward Outlook cards (renderMetalsForward), which is the 'speculators are net long 20.5% of OI' line the owner was actually reading, still showed speculators only. That line now also states the COMMERCIAL net %OI and the plain-language price implication (specs long + commercials short = crowded-long / pullback risk; specs washed-out + commercials buying = bottoming setup), reading macros.metals.cot_{metal}_comm_pct already in the payload. Proven by executing the real line against the live silver payload: 'speculators net long 20.5% of OI ... Commercials ... net short 38.2% of OI -- specs crowded long while hedgers sit short ... pullback risk ...'.

## index v5.348 (display; owner-picked tile grid)

TAB 17 'Your money, drawn' fund-by-fund view rebuilt as a TILE GRID (owner pick), replacing the two-bar-per-fund rows whose light 'this week' bar ran off the right edge (FLXK wk $4,220 overflowed the container). Each fund is now a tile: background colour intensity scales with its since-inception P&L (deep red = biggest loss KSTR, deep green = biggest gain PHPM, relative to the largest absolute move), the dollar figure large, and the weekly figure as a sub-line inside the tile. Responsive auto-fill grid (minmax 150px) so it wraps and can never overflow at any screen width. Same data (pnl_usd + pnl_wow_usd + theme + ticker) and the same K() money formatter; the charges-vs-paid-back balance and footer below are untouched. Audited by executing the real tile renderer against the live payload: 9/9 tiles, both colour ramps present, weekly sub-line on each, no fixed-width bar remaining.

## index v5.347 (display; dashboard-wide jargon glossary)

DASHBOARD-WIDE PLAIN-LANGUAGE JARGON FIX (owner: 'fix all 45'). Audited all ~45 occurrences of chart jargon (death/golden cross, RSI, 50/200-DMA, DMA breadth) and sorted them: many were ALREADY explained (the v5.346 metals technical line; the MA-trend sentence chips at 7000-7048 which build a full plain-English 'title=' deduction; the v5.213 whale-table trend column). The genuinely-bare reader-facing chips are now fixed via one shared helper _jTip(term) returning a plain-English explanation with DIRECTION, wired as a hover title onto: the metals RSI chip, the shared MA-trend chip block (200-day-avg + golden/death cross + RSI), and relabelled table headers ('50-DMA'/'200-DMA' -> '50-day avg'/'200-day avg' with plain tooltips; 'above/below 200-DMA' -> 'above/below 200-day avg'). Proven by executing _jTip across all six terms (rsi/golden/death/200dma/50dma/breadth) -- each returns the correct plain sentence stating overbought/oversold or trend UP/DOWN. Internal code comments and the '200-DMA breadth' scoring-caption (which already carries surrounding plain context) left unchanged. Display-only, tooltips + wording.

## index v5.346 (display; plain-language jargon fix)

METALS TECHNICAL LINE -> PLAIN ENGLISH (owner: 'whats RSI? whats a death cross? does this point price up or down?'). A death cross is in place (the 50-day average has fallen below the 200-day average) -- a signal the trend has turned DOWN over the coming months. METALS COT DEPTH + PLAIN LANGUAGE + GAUGE (owner: seasonality & COT not explained in plain language; non-commercials vs commercials not shown or analysed for price impact; the Forward Outlook graph is boring). scanner v1.442.0: (A) parse_cot_block now captures the COMMERCIAL (producer/hedger) long/short legs from the same CFTC CMX legacy COMMITMENTS block it already fetched -- previously only the non-commercial (speculator) pair was read; cot_{metal}_comm_long/short/net(_pct) added. ## 1.440.0

TIPRANKS ADR ALIAS. v1.439.0's first run stored analyst chips for all 12 US-listed radar names but parse-failed all 6 European ADRs (diag: 200s with no consensus anchor). Third-party investigation proved why: TipRanks keeps TWO OTC lines per European name -- the Y-suffix ADR page carries only earnings/sales forecasts, while the analyst-consensus block lives on the F-suffix foreign-ordinary page. Verified live for Rolls-Royce (rycef/forecast: 'Moderate Buy ... 9 ratings ... average price target is $15.75 ... 3.89% Upside' in exactly the parser's anchored format; rycey/forecast has no such sentence). Fix: when a Y-slug page lacks the consensus anchor, the fetch retries tk[:-1]+'F'; alias hits are recorded in tipranks.diag.alias_used; the five sibling slugs beyond RYCEF follow the documented Y->F convention but are individually unverified, so each retry is FAIL-SOFT -- a miss keeps the honest absence and the legacy partial path (D-126 spirit: unverified paths may be tried, never trusted without the anchors matching). Audited by executing the real overlay against mocked live behaviours: alias hit stored under the radar's own ticker with correct target/upside, dead sibling keeps honest absence, direct US hit unaffected, call order proven with no wasted requests.

## 1.440.0 (redelivered)

TIPRANKS MISS-CACHE (D-122 family) -- caught on v1.439.0's own first live run. REDELIVERY NOTE: the first cut of this version shipped as a version-bumped NO-OP -- the SCAN_VERSION line changed but neither edit was present in the written file (an ambiguous three-way anchor aborted the edit path; D-120 class) -- caught by post-stage verification greps before the owner could deploy it; rebuilt with unique anchors and independent post-write proof of both edits inside fetch_tipranks_overlay. Widening the display-only pool to the radar names exposed that the overlay's two failure paths (HTTP non-200; full parse miss, e.g. bot-shield shells) stored NOTHING, so failed names refetched every single scan forever: the first run's diag showed 1x404 + 6 parse-fails = 7 wasted fetches/scan as a permanent tax, hammering the site's bot defences to boot. Both paths now record {'miss':True,'as_of'} under the same 3-day TTL; the index already renders no TR chip for entries without a consensus, so the display stays unchanged and honest while the refetch tax drops to once per 3 days. Audited by executing the real overlay across all three paths (404 -> cached miss; bot-shell 200 -> cached miss; good page -> full parse) and proving a same-day second run performs ZERO refetches of the misses.

## 1.438.0

POLICY-STAMP PLACEMENT FIX -- own failure, owned. v1.437.0 wired stamp_policy_tickers into mid-main, ~1,000 lines before build_policy_catalyst runs in the tail builders, so every run the stamp found an absent radar, early-returned silently, and shipped 0/28 priced chips with no warning and no px_note -- the exact pipeline-order failure class D-123/D-124 exist to prevent, repeated by placing a call at file-convenience without checking the runtime-order oracle. Caught same-day by auditing the live payload (0/28 + 0.0s stage + missing px_note = the function never saw the data). Fix: the call moves directly after build_policy_catalyst's try block; the empty-radar early-return now logs so it can never be silent again. Delivered with a placement proof (builder line < stamp line asserted on the delivered file) and an execution proof of both orders (absent radar -> visible log; post-builder -> 28/28 stamped).

## 1.436.0

US-GROWTH CLAIMS UNIT FIX -- caught on v1.435.0's own first live payload. FRED serves IC4WSA as raw counts (199,000) while the tile's unit label is K, so the strip displayed '199000K'; claims values are now scaled /1000 at ingest ('199K'). PAYEMS was checked for the same disease and verified ALREADY CORRECT (FRED publishes the payroll level in thousands, so its monthly diff is jobs-in-K by construction -- the live '-23.0K' print was a true soft-July payroll figure, third-party-valid as-is) and deliberately left untouched after an initial over-correction was caught by the audit harness itself. Audited by executing the real builder against FRED-real-shaped raw values.

Audited by executing the real builder across normal / sources-disagree / no-key / partial-series-failure cases (error tiles honest, composite degrades correctly). Pairs with index v5.338 (_usGrowthStrip as the first block of Tab 15's US Recession Watch; sparklines, local esc per v5.218, honest pending line; real-function execution proved strip-first ordering with all six legacy signal cards intact).

## 1.434.0

WAVE P BREADTH CALL-SITE FIX -- the real reason Tab 2's Market Breadth & Leaders panel was blank. The v1.283.0 psx_msci removal deleted the block's opening try:, and because comments are invisible to Python's indentation, the orphaned psx_market lines silently reattached to the PRECEDING bullion except handler, forming a syntactically valid two-except try in which fetch_psx_market_stats only ran if build_psx_bullion RAISED. Bullion never raises -> the breadth fetch was unreachable on every run since v1.283.0. Diagnosis chain: v1.432.0's instrumentation produced the decisive clue by SILENCE (no warning + no data + no 'psx_market' stage timing = the function never executes), then AST proof (Try@24187: body 1 stmt, 2 handlers, the fetch inside handler 1). Fix: one restored try: line. Audited by executing the corrected region: bullion-healthy now fetches (previously impossible), bullion-raising carries bullion and STILL fetches breadth independently, empty-fetch and stage-exception paths carry EXISTING. TV path health itself is still unknown -- v1.432.0's warnings are finally reachable and the next run's meta will speak. Locked as the D-123 family: a removed block's opening statement is part of build correctness; orphaned bodies can reattach silently.

PPI ACTUALS LIVE. _ECON_FRED_MAP extended with 'PPI m/m' -> (PPIFIS, mom_pct) and 'Core PPI m/m' -> (PPIFES, mom_pct). Both ids VERIFIED against FRED's own series pages on 2026-08-14 (PPIFIS = Producer Price Index by Commodity: Final Demand, SA monthly, BLS Producer Price Index release; PPIFES = Final Demand Less Foods and Energy = Core PPI) -- D-126's unverified-id honest blank is lifted for these two titles only. Same mom_pct index-ratio math as CPI; release- and freshness-gates unchanged. Audited by executing the real _enrich_econ_actuals against the live payload's own blank PPI rows (both filled with correct src tags; future-dated and stale-FRED rows proven to stay blank).

## 1.432.0

WAVE P BREADTH INSTRUMENTATION. fetch_psx_market_stats' three failure paths (TV non-200, zero parseable rows, exception) now warn() into meta.warnings -- with HTTP status + response-body head on the non-200 path, which names the rejected column. Why: the stage had failed silently since Wave P; its skip reason went only to the runner log (HTTP 403 to audits), and the {} fallback carried an equally empty EXISTING forever, so Tab 2's Market Breadth & Leaders panel sat blank with no trace in the owner's audit channel. Healthy-path behavior byte-unchanged (proven: full breadth dict, zero warnings). Next run's meta now tells us exactly why TV rejects the call, enabling an evidence-based fix (or the BR-Markets fallback) instead of hit-and-trial.

## 1.431.0

PAYLOAD SOFT-CEILING SENTINEL. Serialize once; if data.json exceeds PAYLOAD_SOFT_CEILING_MB (7.5) the breach + top-3 largest keys are recorded into meta.warnings (the owner's audit channel) and the payload re-serialized so the warning ships in the same run; normal runs stay single-pass (byte-identical output proven on the live payload). Owner-approved 14-Aug after data.json grew 5.7 -> 7.14 MB (im3_detail healing 143 KB/48 tickers -> ~1 MB/234). First live run 2026-08-14T11:40Z: 245.7s, 0 errors, sentinel correctly silent at 7.17 MB.

## 1.430.0

ECON ACTUALS FROM FRED (investigation closed with live proof: the faireconomy weekly export was fetched directly at 11:45 ET, 3h after the CPI release, and its schema carries NO actual field at all -- the old docstring was right, and the v1.429 assumption that actuals appear in-feed was wrong; MT tools with actual values read the FF site itself, which rate-limits exports to 2/5min since Aug-2024). FIX within proven infrastructure: _enrich_econ_actuals() computes actuals from FRED (existing key, existing library) for RELEASED events via a verified-id map only -- CPIAUCSL / CPILFESL (m/m + y/y), ICSA (claims -> 202K format), RSAFS (retail m/m), UMCSENT (sentiment level) -- formatted to match the feed's own strings, gated on release-time passed + observation freshness (45d monthly / 10d weekly). Unverified ids (PPI, core retail, UoM expectations) stay honestly blank rather than guessed. Enrichment mutates the calendar rows in place (the table column fills) then feeds merge_econ_announced (the 14-day log fills). Max ~5 tiny FRED calls/run, usually 0-2. PRIOR: see CHANGELOG.md.

MOAT deep-holdings diag widened. v1.367 showed IVV/ITOT HTTP 200 but bytes=None -- the early 'not _r.text' return fired, so the body is EMPTY on a 200. The diag now ALWAYS records regardless of empty body: http, bytes, content-type, final-url (after redirects), and the first 300 chars -- so one run shows whether it's an HTML redirect page, an empty 200, or a cookie/consent wall. COT FRESHNESS GATE -- cot_futures cost 76.5s this run (vs its ~8-9s norm; CFTC's endpoint was crawling), and the fetch runs twice daily against data CFTC publishes ONCE A WEEK (Fridays, covering Tuesday positions). ## 1.352.0

TIPRANKS PARSE FIX -- v1.351.0's diagnostics delivered the verdict in one run exactly as designed: http={200:24, 404:1}, parse_fail=24, sample_head = a raw-HTML head. The runner is NOT IP-blocked; the pages serve fine. The anchors failed because they were validated against CONVERTED text while the runner receives raw HTML with tags/whitespace interleaved through the very same sentences (e.g. 'consensus rating of <b>Strong Buy</b>'). FIX: preprocess the body -- strip tags (re.sub <[^>]+> -> space), decode the few entities that touch our anchors (&amp; &nbsp; &#36;), collapse whitespace -- THEN apply the SAME already-validated sentence regexes. Belt-and-braces fallback: if sentences still miss, parse n_analysts from the meta-description ('based on N analysts'), which the diag PROVED serves in the raw head. Diagnostics stay. PRIOR: see CHANG

## 1.351.0

(SELF-CORRECTION) TipRanks overlay ran in production and stored ZERO of 25 tickers with ZERO warnings -- because v1.350.0's two skip paths (non-200 response; 200 page that misses the parse anchors) were both silent `continue`s, violating the failures-announce-themselves principle the chunked-scorer fix established. Cannot distinguish runner-IP blocking from anchor drift without evidence. FIX: per-run diagnostics recorded INTO the tipranks payload -- diag = {http: status-code counts, parse_fail count, sample_head: first 160 chars of one 200-but-unparseable body} -- plus one loud warn line whenever picks exist but zero fetches succeed. Next run's data.json names the exact failure mode self-serve. No behavior change on the success path. PRIOR: see CHANGELOG.md.")

(data.json TRIM STEP 2 -- m1_im3_audit metrics slim, the largest safe win found by full consumer census on the live 7.35 MB payload. m1_im3_audit was 412.3 KB for 133 tickers; its per-metric list ({key,verdict,pts,max,val} x ~45) alone was 334 KB. CONSUMERS TRACED BEFORE CUTTING: the index has exactly TWO reads, both the same expression -- ((LIVE.m1_im3_audit[t])||{}).metrics filtered on verdict!=='NA' to compute a thin-coverage ratio -- so the display needs ONLY metrics[].verdict; and the scanner's own M1 cash gate (v1.204.0, verified at the _cash_chain fn) reads aud[t].inputs.cfo/np/fcf and aud[t].is_bank across runs INCLUDING TTL-carried rows -- so inputs/is_bank/pct/grade/iv/src/scored_at all SURVIVE untouched (the gate dependency costs only 57 KB; metrics was the mass). Measured on live data: 412.3 -> 176.0 KB (-236 KB). Tradeoff accepted: the per-metric key/pts detail leaves the audit store; the full WHY remains available on the scored rows' own im3.metrics (explosive_us et al) for any name scored this cycle. TWO edit sites so the store converges in ONE run, not over weeks: (a) the fresh writer now records verdict-only metric entries; (b) the TTL-carry loop re-slims prior-run rows as it copies them (old fat rows would otherwise ride the carry indefinitely). Changed fn: build_m1_buylist region (writer + carry) only.

## 1.344.0

Methodology straight from the canonical docs (Dashboard_Audit2_Strategies_and_BuildPlan.pdf A.3-A.5 + Explosive_IM3_Macro_Framework_Supplement 1.1-1.3), verified against live payload inputs before coding (sbp_rate=11.5, pak_cpi=11.07, arab_light=77.7, econ_clock.us=REFLATION / .psx=STAGFLATION all present). NEW build_sector_selection(data): (1) PSX side -- SBP rate-zone classification (>=11%% = Red/defensive, 9-11 = Yellow/neutral, <9 = Green/constructive) with the doc's five Red-Zone sectors (Banks, E&P, Fertilizer, Utility/Power, Chemicals); five explicit triggers evaluated each run from live macros: A inflation>=11%% -> defensive tilt, B Arab Light>$60 -> E&P overweight (30%% cap noted), C = A+B combo, D rate-cut vs prior run -> banks increase, E rate-hike -> raise T-Bills (D/E read sbp_rate vs EXISTING's prior value -- direction, not level); B/S/G allocation from the doc's backtested table for BOTH risk profiles (Red: HIGH 40/20/40, LOW 50/0/50; Yellow 20/60/20 & 30/40/30; LightGreen 10/80/10 & 25/50/25; DarkGreen 0/100/0 & 15/70/15). (2) US side -- reuses econ_clock.us.quadrant VERBATIM (D-110: a classification shared across surfaces is computed once, never twice) and maps it to the doc's A.4 longs/shorts table (REFLATION -> long Financials/Industrials/Energy/ConsumerDisc/Commodities/EM-FX, short Utilities/Staples/RealEstate/Comms/LongBonds/DXY; all four regimes encoded). Real-rate context (policy minus CPI = +0.43pp now) stamped on the PSX side. Emits data['sector_selection'] = {psx:{zone, zone_color, triggers[], favored[], allocation{high,low}, real_rate_pp, inputs{}}, us:{quadrant, longs[], shorts[], source}} -- pure consolidation, ZERO new fetches, fails soft to EXISTING on any error. Call site placed AFTER econ_clock and sector_booming (verified line order 22409 -> 22443 -> here), inputs proven to exist at the call point per the pipeline-validation rule. Changed: main() call site; added: build_sector_selection.

## 1.343.0

(THE REAL ROOT CAUSE, found by reading the owner's pasted run log line by line: it showed ZERO '[Signal T fail]' lines despite XOM/CCNE still being confirmed empty in sigt_quarterly_cache -- meaning v1.341.0's diagnostic code never even ran for them. Traced to the warm-cache check `if bare in _SAT_Q_CACHE: return _SAT_Q_CACHE[bare]` -- XOM and CCNE were cached as [] from a run that predates v1.340.0's Pretax-Income fallback entirely, and 'bare in dict' is True for an EMPTY list just as much as a real one. Every run since -- including the ones carrying v1.340.0's fix and v1.341.0's diagnostics -- has returned that stale pre-fix empty result immediately, WITHOUT EVER REACHING the fetch code, so neither the fallback logic nor the failure-reason tracking ever got a chance to run on them. This explains everything: VINP/BX worked because their cache entries didn't predate the fix; XOM/CCNE (and an estimated ~216 other tickers per the last full cache read) never will, no matter how many times the fetch logic itself gets improved, because the cache masks it. FIX: the warm-cache hit now requires the cached value to be NON-EMPTY -- an empty result is no longer treated as 'done', it always gets a real retry attempt. Bounded to avoid a runtime blowup (retrying ~216 previously-empty tickers every run would add real time): a new _SIGT_EMPTY_RETRY_BUDGET=30 caps fresh retry attempts per run, same pattern as this file's existing _QA_QHIST_BUDGET=25 for the identical tradeoff. Once budget is exhausted for a run, remaining previously-empty tickers fall back to the old cached result for that run only, not permanently. Changed fn: _sa_quarterly_op.

## 1.342.0

(SELF-CORRECTION -- v1.341.0's _SIGT_FAIL_REASONS diagnostic was only ever logged to the console, never persisted into data.json. Confirmed live: pulled the deployed data.json after v1.341.0's first real run (374.8s, 0 errors, had_data 277->286, fired 165->170 -- genuine incremental progress) and found sigt_stats carried only the pre-existing aggregate counters, with no trace of the new per-reason breakdown. The whole point of building that diagnostic was to self-serve WHY XOM/CCNE still fail without needing the owner to paste a console log again -- logging it to console only defeats that purpose, since GitHub Actions logs are 403 from here. FIX: data['sigt_fail_reasons'] = dict(_SIGT_FAIL_REASONS), stamped alongside the existing sigt_stats write, same try/except-guarded pattern. No change to v1.340.0/v1.341.0's actual fetch, fallback, or diagnostic-recording logic -- purely adds the missing persistence step. Changed: one line added next to the existing sigt_stats write.

## 1.341.0

(DIAGNOSTIC VISIBILITY for the 2 remaining Signal-T fetch failures -- owner: 'fix it', re: XOM/CCNE still returning empty quarterly series after the v1.340.0 Pretax-Income fallback landed. Investigated first rather than guessing: CCNE's REAL income statement (confirmed via external sources) uses the exact label 'Pretax Income' already in v1.340.0's fallback list -- so this is NOT a label-matching bug, ruling out the most obvious next guess. The real gap: _sa_quarterly_op()'s bare `except Exception: out = []` swallows EVERY failure mode identically -- an HTTP error, a timeout, a parser exception, and 'the page loaded fine but no row matched either label set' are all indistinguishable in the output and in the run log. Cannot diagnose blind from a sandbox that has stockanalysis.com blocked (the function's own docstring confirms this). FIX: adds _SIGT_FAIL_REASONS, a per-run counter keyed by the ACTUAL failure mode (http_<code>, timeout, request_exception, parse_exception, no_matching_row) recorded for every ticker whose fetch comes back empty, plus which specific tickers hit each reason (capped at 10 names per reason to keep the log bounded). Surfaced in the existing Signal-T summary log line so the NEXT real run shows exactly why XOM/CCNE (and any other empty result) failed, instead of one aggregate number that can't distinguish a genuine 'no data' from a fixable request-level problem. Zero behaviour change to the fetch or fallback logic itself -- v1.340.0's fix is unmodified; this only adds the missing visibility needed to take the next real corrective step instead of guessing further. Changed fns: _sa_quarterly_op (diagnostics only); the explosive_us Signal-T summary log line.

## 1.340.0

(ROOT-CAUSE FIX for signal_t's Finance-sector coverage gap -- owner asked to investigate why VINP/XOM/BX/CCNE showed no turnaround streak, then find and fix the DISEASE, not just these 4 stocks). Root cause confirmed at the code level: _sa_quarterly_op() only recognises rows literally labelled 'operating income'/'operating income (loss)'/'operating profit' -- stockanalysis.com's STANDARD non-financial template. Banks, asset managers and PE firms (confirmed live: Blackstone serves under a 'Capital Markets template', banks under a 'Financial Services template') report Net Interest Income / Provision for Credit Losses / Pretax Income instead and carry NO row matching those three labels at all -- so the parser silently returns [] for the entire Finance sector, not isolated tickers. Measured before fixing: Finance-sector signal_t coverage was 27%% (15/55) vs 51%% (150/295) everywhere else -- roughly HALF the rate, confirming a systemic, sector-wide gap rather than a few unlucky misses. XOM (not Finance-sector) was independently verified as a CORRECT no-fire -- real multi-year operating-income decline, no turnaround exists to detect -- and is untouched by this fix. FIX: _sa_quarterly_op() now falls back to a Pretax-Income family label ('pretax income', 'income before taxes', 'income before income taxes', 'pre-tax income') when no Operating-Income row is found -- a line virtually every company reports under every stockanalysis.com template regardless of sector, and the closest available proxy for the same profitability-inflection signal signal_t exists to detect. The series is tagged _proxy_line so any future consumer can tell a true Operating-Income series from the fallback. Considered and deferred: swapping the whole series to TradingView's quarterly EPS history (TV already supplies q_eps_g/q_rev_g for these exact 4 tickers via growth_source='tv', so TV clearly has SOME quarterly coverage here) -- not built this pass because it needs a live probe of whether TV's scan API actually serves INDEXED HISTORICAL quarters (not just the current one) before writing production code against it, per this file's own capture-first rule; flagged as the stronger long-term fix. Changed fn: _sa_quarterly_op only.

Measured on live data before fixing: 21 of the 40 current Tab19 picks carried this false credit; simulated the fix precisely -- 24 survive on genuine engines, 16 (PBF/DK/CDNL/LGN/STRL/DINO/UNIT/VCTR/XOM/CVX/ENB/SM/GLNG/AGNC/SHLS/MVBF) drop below the 2-engine bar and are correctly removed. (2) BANK ZACKS COVERAGE: measured 8 of the 9 is_bank=True US tickers (TOWN/CARE/CCNE/ISTR/PLBC/MCBS/OSBC/MCHB, all classified into the separate bank-model 'FINANCIAL' verdict) had NO Zacks rank at all -- traced to fetch_zacks_sectors's survivors argument, which is built from the growth-screen candidate pool; is_bank names exit that pipeline into the separate bank-model path before this point and were never being passed in. Fixed by explicitly merging every is_bank explosive_us row into the survivors list at the call site (verified pipeline order: explosive_us builds at the earlier line, so this data is available). (3) BANKS-TAB SOURCE: build_recommended gains a genuine, gated 'Banks-tab' source reading the SAME is_bank/is_financial pool the Banks tab itself renders (Finance-sector explosive_us rows, banks and non-bank financials both) -- tagged distinctly for traceability per the owner's ask, subject to the SAME standing >=2-engine rule as every other source; being on the Banks tab is not on its own a merit signal, it only ever supplies ONE tag, same as any other source. ## 1.333.0

(OUTPERFORMANCE GATE for the consensus's Turnaround and small-cap sources -- owner: 'the turnarounds and small caps performance should be above the sector and the indices average'. TWO additions. (1) fetch_us_index_returns(): ONE proven TVC symbols-POST (identical pattern to the RUT/econ fetches) for TVC:SPX/TVC:NDX/TVC:DJI Perf.YTD + Perf.Y -> data['index_returns'] = {sp500,nasdaq,dow:{ytd,ret_1y}}; best-effort, {} on any failure, never blocks the run. (2) _sector_avg_perf(): computes each GICS/TV sector's median YTD from foundation_universe (already-fetched perf_ytd, zero new fetch) -- the honest baseline a stock needs to beat. build_recommended's Turnaround and small-cap sources (small-cap = a pick whose only YTD comes from exmap, i.e. it falls below foundation_universe's $2bn floor) are now GATED: kept ONLY when ytd_pct exceeds BOTH its sector's median YTD AND every available index's YTD (SPX/NDX/DJI) -- names that fail either bar are dropped from the consensus, and every surviving pick carries vs_sector_pct/vs_sp500_pct/vs_nasdaq_pct/vs_dow_pct so the outperformance is visible, not just implied. FAILS OPEN by design: a name whose sector or index comparison data is unavailable (e.g. the index fetch missed) is KEPT with the comparison fields None rather than silently dropped -- a data gap must never empty the consensus. Core Explosive/TCE-HIGH/M1-Buy/Zacks/Whale/Multibagger/Discovery sources are UNCHANGED -- this gate applies only to the two sources v1.332.0 just added, per the owner's specific ask. Changed fn: build_recommended; added: fetch_us_index_returns, _sector_avg_perf.

## 1.331.0

(W28 BEST/WORST DASH FIX -- owner asked why the first statement week still shows a dash. ROOT CAUSE, proven from the live li.history store: the per-fund price map (px) is empty on the inception days (2026-07-06/07 carry NAV only; the first px-bearing snapshot is 2026-07-08), and the uploaded IBKR statements do NOT contain a 2026-07-06 daily CLOSE either (the Activity Statement's Prior price column is '--' because June-30 qty was 0). The old code took snaps[0] literally, so W28's first snapshot had no px -> best=worst=None -> dash. FIX: best/worst now measures from the FIRST PRICE-BEARING snapshot in the period (and to the last price-bearing one), using the real recorded prices that DO exist, and stamps px_start/px_end dates so the row is honest about the window it measured. A NOTE is NOT fabricated: no synthetic entry price is injected (the px map mixes native + USD-converted currencies per fund, so seeding cost would mis-scale ITWN/KSTR). Any future single-day gap now heals the same way. Only best/worst changes; period NAV/pnl/since-inception are untouched. Changed fn: build_li_statements.

## 1.330.0

(LIVE-INVESTMENT TV TECHNICAL RATING -- owner asked for TradingView analyst ratings against each holding. ETFs are NOT rated by sell-side analysts (only their underlying stocks are, which the look-through layer already covers), but TradingView publishes a TECHNICAL rating (recommendation_mark, 1=Strong Buy .. 3=Strong Sell) for the fund's own price action, and fetch_etf_recommendations already reads it. resolve_etf_live_price now ALSO requests recommendation_mark and returns it as tv_reco, and build_live_investment stamps tv_reco + tv_reco_label (Strong Buy/Buy/Neutral/Sell/Strong Sell) onto every holding. Additive, one extra column on an existing POST, no new fetch; a fund TV does not rate stays tv_reco=None -> label 'n/a', byte-identical elsewhere. Changed fns: resolve_etf_live_price, build_live_investment.

## 1.328.0

(ROLL-FREE OIL TREND BASIS -- fixed INSIDE the scanner, no external dependency, owner-instructed after three failed attempts to reach TradingView's continuous CFDs. THE DEFECT, visible in today's live data: brent_mom = -25.68% while brent_spot_mom = +30.87%, and wti_mom = -27.24% while wti_spot_mom = +15.94%. The _mom/_wow/_qoq fields are derived from FRONT-MONTH FUTURES prints (CL1!/BRN1!), whose contract ROLLS inject step-changes that are not market moves -- the exact -25.7% artefact v1.314.7 first identified. The v1.314.7 one-time history purge could not cure it because the continuous replacement NEVER ARRIVED: TVC:USOIL/UKOIL return rows=0 from every scan universe and null from the symbol GET, so the store kept filling with futures prints. THE FIX USES WHAT ALREADY WORKS: v1.314.9 already fetches FRED's official EIA continuous SPOT series (DCOILWTICO / DCOILBRENTEU), which is roll-immune BY CONSTRUCTION and is already correct in today's data. _apply_rollfree_oil_trends() now re-bases wti/brent _wow/_mom/_qoq onto that spot series when it is available, recomputes each _dir from the re-based number, stamps {key}_trend_basis, and PRESERVES the old futures reads as {key}_{span}_futures for forensics. _spot_qoq is added (64 trading days) so the quarter read is covered too. The LEVEL is untouched -- the futures print is not corrupted, only the change-over-time was. Spot missing -> every field is byte-identical to v1.327.0. Changed fn: fetch_us_macros; added: _apply_rollfree_oil_trends.

## 1.327.0

(SYMBOL-GET ENDPOINT -- root cause found by the owner's DevTools capture, not by reasoning. TVC:USOIL/TVC:UKOIL are LIVE on TradingView (owner charted them at 84.06) but return rows=0 from EVERY /scan universe, while TVC:DXY resolves through the identical call in the same run -- the /scan route simply does not index commodity CFDs. The capture shows tradingview.com itself reading these symbols with a GET: scanner.tradingview.com/symbol?symbol=TVC%3ADXY&fields=...&no_404=true&label-product=symbols-performance, and the response is a FLAT JSON OBJECT keyed by the requested field names ({'Perf.1M':6.33,'change':-2.14,...}) with no data/s/d wrapper. THE ORIGINAL SIN: this scanner already found /symbol at v1.158.0, sent it a POST, got HTTP 405 Method Not Allowed, and concluded the endpoint was wrong -- one wrong verb, and every oil workaround since descends from it. FIX: new fetch_tv_symbol_quote() does the captured GET and parses the captured flat shape; fetch_tv_oil tries it FIRST for the continuous crude CFDs, falls back to the v1.326.0 scan ladder for anything still missing, then to the NYMEX:CL1!/ICEEUR:BRN1! futures path exactly as before. Diags name the endpoint, the HTTP code and the returned keys, so a miss is self-explaining. Total failure is byte-identical to v1.326.0 (futures serve, nothing blanks). Changed fn: fetch_tv_oil; added: fetch_tv_symbol_quote.

## 1.326.0

(OIL CONTINUOUS UNIVERSE LADDER -- owner-instructed, the same class of bug v1.325.0 just fixed and the same remedy, now VALIDATED IN PRODUCTION: the 19:15 run logged '[TV econ ok] universe=global serving core_pce, cpi_yoy, fed_rate, ppi_core_yoy, unemployment', so the global universe WITH A MARKETS PAYLOAD resolves a namespace the market-path universes cannot. TVC:USOIL / TVC:UKOIL have been returning HTTP 200 rows=0 from cfd/scan every run (two standing warnings), leaving Brent/WTI continuous on last-good. FIX: (a) fetch_index_tv now accepts a market entry as EITHER a plain string (path-only -- byte-identical for every existing caller, all of which pass strings) OR a ('path', {payload-extras}) tuple, which is the exact form that worked for ECONOMICS; (b) fetch_tv_oil walks _TV_OIL_UNIVERSES = cfd -> america -> global+markets[cfd] -> global+markets[futures], stopping at the first that resolves; (c) the standing diag now names EVERY universe tried with its status/rows/body-head into meta.warnings, so if it still misses the next run says exactly which universe answered what -- no guessing cycle. NYMEX:CL1!/ICEEUR:BRN1! futures fallback and the whole roll-free history contract are untouched; all universes failing is byte-identical to v1.325.0. Changed fns: fetch_index_tv, fetch_tv_oil.

## 1.325.0

(TV ECON UNIVERSE FIX -- owner-instructed. The v1.324.0 FRED->TV level swap has been silently INACTIVE since it shipped: fetch_tv_econ POSTed ECONOMICS: tickers to scanner.tradingview.com/america/scan, the US EQUITY universe, which cannot resolve them, so _tv_pre stayed {} and every run fell back to FRED byte-identically. PROOF (offline, no log needed): the TV branch stamps out[key+'_source']='tradingview' and NO macro key in the deployed data.json carries that field, while core_pce/ppi_core_yoy/unemployment all still carry the fresh FRED _mom/_qoq/_yoy trend fields that the TV path deliberately skips. FIX: fetch_tv_econ now tries an ordered CANDIDATE LADDER of scan universes in ONE run -- global/scan with markets=[economics2], then economics2/scan, then the current america/scan last -- stopping at the first that returns a usable value. Every attempt emits a capped self-naming diag through warn() into meta.warnings (Actions logs are 403, so meta IS the runner log), and success emits one [TV econ ok] line naming the winning universe. ALL candidates failing is byte-identical to today: {} -> FRED serves everything, no regression. TV_ECON_PRIMARY=True remains the one-line revert. Changed fn: fetch_tv_econ only; _TV_ECON_UNIVERSES added.
