Prior 1.305.0: (METALS FORECAST LAYER, owner: 'analyst predictions and engine predictions with trends are missing ... will price increase or decline in 1 or 2 months'.) THREE additions to every metal card: (a) PER-DRIVER TREND -- each driver now carries its own week/month/quarter change plus an invert flag, so the tab shows whether that pressure is BUILDING or EASING rather than just its level; (b) ENGINE PROJECTION -- _proj_range() derives a 1-month and 2-month price range from the metal's OWN realised volatility (90-day series) tilted by the driver composite, stated as a statistical range, never a target; (c) ANALYST CONSENSUS -- METAL_ANALYST_VIEW carries dated, sourced institutional targets (gold: GS $4,900, JPM $4,500, DB $4,800, SSGA $5,500, range $4,400-5,500; silver: LBMA 26-analyst consensus $79.57, JPM $81, HSBC $75, GS $85-100, Commerzbank $90) with the gap to spot computed live -- a manual sourced config, the same honest pattern as SBP reserves, refreshed at the mid-Oct ritual. Prior 1.304.0: (PER-METAL DRIVER MODELS -- the owner's correction: 'what about silver? is ISM/LSM not a big factor for silver?'. He is right and it is THE distinction in this asset class: gold is monetary (industrial use ~5%% of demand) so real yields/dollar/Fed/inflation set it, while SILVER is ~50-60%% industrial (solar PV alone ~29%% of demand, up from ~11%% in 2014) so FACTORY ACTIVITY is its demand engine AND it inherits gold's macro in amplified form. One shared board was therefore wrong. Now five per-metal models with research-grounded weights -- gold: real yield x3, dollar x2, inflation x2, Fed x2; silver: industrial production x3, real yield x2, dollar x2, gold-silver ratio x2; copper/platinum/palladium: industrial x3 first. Each metal returns split favouring/pressuring lists, a composite, a confidence read and a 1-2 month DIRECTIONAL outlook (explicitly a bias, never a target). not_covered now also names solar-PV thrifting and vault inventories. Prior 1.303.0: (METALS DRIVER BOARD + ETF PERF SANITY GUARD, owner-directed.) (1) build_metals_drivers scores what RESEARCH says actually sets the gold price -- real yields (dominant: gold's opportunity cost), the dollar, Fed policy, inflation, COT positioning as an amplifier, and price trend -- ALL from data already fetched (us_10y minus breakeven_10y gives the real yield; DXY, fed_rate, cpi_yoy, cot_*_pct, *_px_ma_trend/rsi14). Emits weighted per-driver scores, a -100..+100 composite, a plain-language bias, and an explicit not_covered list (central-bank buying, ETF flows, geopolitical risk) so the gaps are stated rather than implied. Seasonality stays where it is. (2) _etf_perf_sane rejects impossible fund returns after the owner caught an EM IG BOND fund showing 729.2%% YTD with 6m/3m within 4 points -- the signature of a PRICE in a perf field (that fund quotes in GBp; its ISIN was also wrong, BE... on a Luxembourg SICAV). Prior 1.302.0: (AUDIT GAPS 1-2.) (a) E&P TRIGGER: Brent/WTI were fetched correctly but NOTHING acted on them while the owner's standing rule is 'Arab Light > $60 = overweight E&P' and he holds XOM/CVX/COP/EOG. build_ep_signal derives an HONEST Arab Light proxy (Brent minus a stated $2 OSP differential -- no free live Arab Light feed exists, so it is labelled a proxy, never an exact print) and emits data['ep_signal'] with fired/action/names/grades. Live: Brent 100.3 -> proxy 98.3 -> FIRED. (b) Paired daily.yml adds the owner's 10 US holdings to the IM3 universe -- XOM/COP/EOG/UNH/GIS had no grade anywhere. NOTE: the audit's third 'gap' (Tab 7 empty) was a PROBE ERROR -- the tab renders 6,004 chars inline from macros with all sub-blocks populated. Prior 1.301.0: (SIZE PASS -- float rounding at the write boundary.) data.json stored 16-digit floats where 4 decimals is all any display shows; _round_floats() rounds only inside json.dump, so in-run maths keeps full precision and nothing rendered changes. Pairs with the daily.yml grade-book slim (1.81 -> 0.13 MB). Prior 1.299.0: (CROSS-TAB SCORED-EVERYWHERE PASS, owner-approved.) (1) NARRATIVE PROPAGATION: a stock inside a live news theme showed nothing on any other tab, breaking the cross-tab rule -- every explosive/TCE/M2/pink record a live theme names now carries r['narr'] {themes[], sent, t1} and the index badges it. (2) Paired daily.yml adds the 13F whale top-holdings + Wave-Z consensus names to the IM3 universe (~54 net new): the dashboard scored 190 names the owner does not own and none that smart money holds. NOTE (audited, not assumed): all 7 live_investment holdings are UCITS ETFs -- IM3 is an equity model and cannot grade a fund, so they are NOT added; the fund-quality view is a separate decision. Prior 1.298.0: (TURNAROUND VERDICT TIER, owner-directed after the DELL audit: 'is it not marked turnaround?'.) The verdict was computed from Signals A+B ONLY -- Q (quarterly early-warning) and T (rolling-year profit turning up) were displayed but never classified, so an inflecting name fell to NOT EXPLOSIVE and into the HIDDEN section. DELL: revenue +87.5%% this quarter, TTM operating profit up 8 consecutive quarters (4,909->10,640), annual 18.8%% -> buried. 23 names shared the profile. Now: NOT EXPLOSIVE + Signal Q + Signal T fired = 'TURNAROUND \u2014 quarterly inflection (NQ profit rising)'. Upgrade-only (no existing verdict changes); index v5.251 gives it a loud, visible amber tier. Prior 1.297.1: (CACHE INVALIDATION for the v1.297.0 fix -- caught by the post-deploy audit: DELL still read 374.4 because explosive_stmt_cache REPLAYED its 18-Jul entry (src=sec_edgar, v=sec3) and the corrected code never ran for cached names. EXPLOSIVE_CACHE_SCHEMA sec3->sec4 invalidates all 452 stale entries -> one heavier run (statement re-fetch), then normal. Prior 1.297.0: (DELL GROWTH ROOT CAUSE -- SEC annual-series duration guard.) Owner-audited: DELL showed rev_growth 374.4%% / op 277.4%% / np 287.2%% against verified truth of FY 18.8%% / ~32%% / 29.3%% (quarterly 87.5%% and EPS 282%% were CORRECT -- the EXPLOSIVE verdict is genuinely earned; the AI-server ramp is real). Cause: _sec_annual_series admitted any fact with form='10-K' and fp='FY', but a 10-K also publishes QUARTERLY-duration facts under those same labels; the later entry overwrote the true full-year value, so _yoy divided an annual column by a quarterly one (~4-5x inflation across revenue/OI/NI/PBT). Fix: flow facts carry start+end -- require a 300-400 day span; instant facts without 'start' keep the original test. Blast radius audited: 16/350 names showed the pattern but 15 are legitimate micro-caps scaling from near-zero revenue; DELL was the only large-cap and the only EXPLOSIVE verdict affected. Prior 1.296.0: (MINIFY data.json -- owner-approved highest-return one-liner.) The final write used indent=2, spending ~5.6MB of the ~12MB file on indentation whitespace alone; separators=(',',':') with no indent halves the file (~6.3MB) with byte-identical JSON.parse output -- nothing downstream changes. Paired daily.yml minifies its three post-scan re-writes too (else they'd re-inflate). Prior 1.295.0: (NARRATIVES TAB DEFECTS from owner's rendered paste -- audited, not assumed.) (1) SEC 8-K + insider rows showed '? . .': _recent_events read a flat-list shape but mat_events/insider_events are dicts keyed by TICKER -> {events:[...]}; now flattened correctly with the ticker stamped from the key. (2) Off-topic themes (Metals showed chip/SpaceX/Uber Reuters headlines): site:reuters.com with a broad phrase returned the publisher's general feed -- scoped queries now quote the theme's core token AND wire headlines mentioning none of the theme's tokens are dropped. Core topic tokens added per theme. Prior 1.294.0: (NARRATIVES LIVE-RUN FIXES -- the owner's tab paste exposed three defects, each proven from data.) (1) FRESHNESS: the tariffs theme fetched 270 items and kept ZERO -- Google relevance-ranking serves mostly >14d items for policy queries; '+when:14d' now forces recency at the source on every query. (2) FLOOD: general-query items filled ents[:60] before the scoped Reuters/Bloomberg entries were reached (100 items each, none surviving); scoped T1 + direct feeds now precede the general aggregate. (3) QUALITY SCORE: a zero-T1 crypto-blog cluster topped the tab at 24 over Nikkei/Reuters themes; the score is now T1-dominated (t1*6 + t2*2 + capped breadth). ALSO DISCOVERED via the tab: DELL carries rev_growth 374.4%% (TV-sourced) vs a real-world ~8-10%% -- a pre-existing Explosive-tab data defect, flagged for a sanity-guard build. Prior 1.293.0: (BROKER & ANALYST LAYER, owner: 'why not international securities and brokers news feed?'.) Primary bulge-bracket research (GS/MS/JPM notes) is a regulated client-only product with no public feeds (MiFID-II unbundling) -- but its public EXHAUST is free and now wired: (a) MarketBeat's genuine analyst-ratings RSS joins the direct feeds; (b) NEW 'Broker & Analyst Calls' theme aggregates upgrade/downgrade/PT actions naming the firms, with affected stocks EXTRACTED from the (EXCH:TICKER) patterns the headlines carry -- a 'Citigroup upgrades Micron (NASDAQ:MU)' line stamps MU automatically; (c) NEW 'PSX Broker Research' theme surfaces the mandated JS/AKD/Topline houses via indexed coverage. NOTE: the STRUCTURED form of international broker research was already live -- TV recommendation marks (s12), price-target upside (s11), and the 623-name estimate-revisions engine. Prior 1.292.0: (NARRATIVES SOURCE-LAYER UPGRADE, owner: 'why not bloomberg? your resources are not the top ones' -> 'first list all reliable news resources and then build the code'.) Verified: Reuters' public RSS is dead, Bloomberg/WSJ/FT have no official free feeds -- the legal free route to T1 headlines is Google News SCOPED queries. Every theme now fans out to 3 queries (general + site:reuters.com + site:bloomberg.com, concurrent), PLUS four direct official publisher feeds (CNBC Top/Markets/Tech, MarketWatch Top) fetched once per run and keyword-routed into themes; scoped/direct headlines carry authoritative source tags (Reuters/Bloomberg/CNBC/MarketWatch -> correct T1/T2 badges), cross-source dedup added, T1 headlines sort first, and per-feed item counts persist in narratives['source_health'] so reachability audits itself every run. SEC 8-K + Fed RSS + insiders were already fused (the true T1 layer). ~37 concurrent RSS fetches, +8-15s. Prior 1.291.0: (NARRATIVES ENGINE, owner-directed: 'make a narratives tab and score it with explosive metrics all of them, let tce be independent'.) NEW build_narratives: 11 THEME-level Google News feeds (tariffs/White House/AI/semis/rates/energy/metals/crypto/pharma/Pakistan/defense) through the proven _fetch_rss_entries plumbing -- policy stories that never name a ticker now map onto the watch universe (explosive + TCE + pink + holdings) via sector exposure + explicit lists (the Trump-news-for-DELL gap closed). Rule-based classification: event bucket, sentiment polarity, source tier (T1 Reuters/Bloomberg/WSJ...). Fuses the already-collected SEC 8-K mat_events + insider_events as premium news rows. Keep-last-good on total feed failure. TCE untouched (frozen to Sept read-out); the index (v5.249) renders each theme's affected stocks with the FULL explosive metric set by joining existing records -- zero rescoring. Prior 1.290.0: (WAVE-Z ORDER FIX.) The rebuilt consensus box instrumented its own bug on run one: whales_used=0 with whale_13f fresh in the same file -- the Wave-Z block executes BEFORE build_whale_13f in main(), reading a key not yet built THIS run. Whale source now falls back to EXISTING (holdings stable day-to-day); the box fills from last run's filings -- correct semantics. Prior 1.289.0: (THREE-ITEM AUDIT PASS, owner-approved.) (1) WAVE-Z v2: the Tab-9 consensus box sat empty forever -- root-caused to its ETF-holdings leg (fetch_etf_holdings, FMP-premium-dead 0/30 since ~v1.252) starving compute_inst_consensus while the fund leg worked (11 funds live). Dead leg retired; second confirmation now = 13F WHALE top holdings (live daily, ticker-stamped v1.242): >=2 funds AND >=2 whales = double-confirmed; basis field states the change. (2) SIGT-STATS TRUTH FIX: the persist sat at the EARLY meta-init anchor, snapshotting 0/0/0 before run_explosive populated the counters -- the evaluator was healthy all along; snapshot moved to end-of-run. (3) PINK PROXY PRICE-JOIN: 0/50 pink proxies carried live perf; every already-priced ISIN in data (ETF watches + explosive/tce priced proxies) now stamps ytd/ret_1y/daily_chg onto matching pink rows -- zero new fetching, ucitsPerf renders automatically. Index v5.248 pairs (consensus renderer: whales replace the dead ETF column; empty-state honesty). Prior 1.288.0: (MICRO-BUILD: the forensics-named TypeError + FOMC self-explanation.) (1) meta['swallowed'] named its first defect within one run: closes = [...] if len(h) -- h stays None BY DESIGN on the _PSX_YAHOO_SKIP path, so the two skip-list pool names crashed the whole per-name stream block every run (the mysterious 2 swallows/run, ~since v1.112). Guarded (h is not None and len(h)); the designed TV-perf fallback now actually engages. (2) fomc null forever with no explanation: when the Fed RSS yields nothing and no last-good exists, out['fomc_status'] says so, and the feed exception now registers in meta['swallowed'] as fomc.fetch -- next run confirms whether the runner can reach federalreserve.gov at all (old-backlog confirm item closed either way). Prior 1.287.0: (OFFICIAL-HEADLINE TE CHAIN + SWALLOW FORENSICS + UNTIMED TIMERS.) (1) CPI headline chain gains its second link: TV USIRYY missed two consecutive live runs, so TradingEconomics (fetch_te_econ, proven every scan; the inflation-cpi page carrying the announced rate) now tries before falling back to FRED's index-derived value; cpi_diag records official_te + displayed. (2) The 750s network event proved silent tce.fetch retries a live cost: _swallow now records exception class + seconds burned per swallow, persisted to data['meta']['swallowed'] (was print-only behind the 403 log). (3) Three untimed blocks (carry-forward, radar/ETF-X/whale cluster, final JSON write) join timings_ms so the ~85s untimed gap attributes itself. Prior 1.286.0: (THE FULL-RESCORE ROOT CAUSE -- workflow-state carry-through.) PROVEN by the owner's 15-minute-job audit: the scanner rebuild DROPPED the workflow's state keys (im3_last_scored_utc, im3_scorer_hash, im3_errors, im3_stamp_reason) every scan, and the trigger's staleness check defaults stale=True when the clock is missing -> EVERY run full-rescored ~190 names since the ceiling was introduced. The hash-file theories were red herrings; the durable-hash design was correct but this deletion erased the state before the trigger read it. All four keys now carry via the same block that already preserves im3_explosive_tickers. ALSO: cpi_diag now records official_tv_status when the TV headline is unavailable/crashed, so the override's absence explains itself (last run: tile stayed 3.88 with silent empty diag -- TV had no value that run). Prior 1.285.0: (CPI OFFICIAL-HEADLINE OVERRIDE -- the evidence-dictated fix.) cpi_diag's first run PROVED the FRED arithmetic correct on FRED's own NSA index (321.465 -> 333.952 = 3.8844%) while the officially announced June rate is 3.5% -- source mismatch, not math. The tile now displays the announced rate itself (TV ECONOMICS:USIRYY, one batched POST through the proven symbols pattern) when available and sane (0-25%% band); FRED stays for trend math + fallback; cpi_diag records fred_computed AND official_tv so the audit trail is permanent. Prior 1.284.0: (CPI ARITHMETIC DIAGNOSTIC + SIGT CACHE-HIT COUNTERS.) (1) The CPI tile read 3.88 after the NSA swap vs the official 3.5 headline -- BOTH bases tried, neither matches, so per the no-guess rule the next fix must come from evidence: macros.us.cpi_diag now persists the FRED series id, the last 14 raw index points, the exact division pair and the computed value -- one run names the defect. (2) _SIGT_STATS gains cache_hits: with 486 cached series the counters legitimately read 0/0/0 (they only counted fetches) which looked broken; warm-cache serving is now visible. Prior 1.283.0: (WAVE FIX-A -- independent-source audit corrections, owner-approved.) (1) CPI HEADLINE BASIS: the FRED fallback computed YoY on the SA series (CPIAUCSL) -> tile showed 3.73%% vs the official June headline 3.5%% every news source quotes; fallback now uses CPIAUCNS (headline NSA). (2) SIGNAL-T SELF-REPORTING: explosive-lane _SIGT_STATS persists to data['sigt_stats'] and the M2 lane's coverage (+ the T-less shown turnarounds BY NAME) rides data['m2_watch']['sigt_stats'] -- diagnosis no longer lives only behind the 403-walled Actions log. (3) psx_msci dead key retired (block + fetch_psx_msci + default; shelved since v1.76.0, emitted {} every run). Workflow pairs: data['im3_stamp_reason'] records why the 14-day clock stamped. Prior 1.282.0: (M1 ZACKS-DISCOVERY LANE, owner-approved.) A-grade pink-tier discoveries join build_m1_buylist's candidate pool as a fourth lane (quick-score + mcap + Zacks-#1 + NOW discovery) -- live evidence: C/GOOGL/SCHW/ADP/DIS/FOXA/WFC were Grade A yet invisible to M1. No free pass: same internal deep score, same cash-conversion gate, same rank; non-favored sectors flagged off_regime; src='zacks_discovery' provenance on pool + scored records. Prior 1.281.0: (PINK-TIER COMPLETION PASS, owner-approved four items.) (a) every pink row resolves its UCITS proxy via resolve_ucits_proxy_for_stock -- catalog-only, zero fetch -- so discoveries carry the same estate-tax-safe Irish-ETF route as every other row; (b) a discovery below the $2bn foundation floor (MGNX-class) carries an explicit note instead of blank cells; (c) the radar payload surfaces zacks_scraped_utc so the tab can show how old the weekly ranks are; (d) _M2_SIGT_BUDGET 40->80 -- the quarterly cache serves most lookups free, the old cap was limiting turnaround-lane coverage (13/20) not cost. Index v5.246 pairs (scrape-age line, floor-note under name; proxy line renders automatically). Prior 1.280.0: (PINK TIER JOINS THE IM3 SCORING SET, owner-directed: 'all zacks 1 and 2 should be im scored as well' -- the v1.279.0 live audit showed 0/60 discovery names carried a grade because the workflow's scoring set collects by verdict and the pink tier is outside every collected verdict.) THREE-FILE change, deploy together: daily.yml collects zacks_radar rows + zacks_discovery-stamped records into the scoring set (one-time ~60-name scoring pass, then incremental), searches radar rows in the self-heal, and merges scores into the radar rows; THIS scanner carries each pink row's im3 forward across rebuilds (keep-last-good: current-ver clean, ver-mismatch marked stale_ver for the amber chip + workflow heal); index v5.244 loads radar-row grades into im3Scores and treats the ZACKS verdict as in-scope (Pending until scored, never a dead dash). Prior 1.279.0: (ZACKS DISCOVERIES AS PINK FULL-DATA TABLE ROWS, owner-directed: 'after yellow in pink background filling all columns as green and yellow are doing'.) build_zacks_radar v2: instead of a chip strip, every Zacks #1/#2 name off the main board becomes a FULL row in the Explosive table -- screened NOT-EXPLOSIVE radar names are stamped zacks_discovery=True (the index lifts their real records out of the hidden tier into the pink tier), and outside-screen names get explosive-shaped records built from the ~1,900-name foundation universe already in data.json (real TV name/sector/price/Rev-YoY-TTM/EPS-YoY/YTD/1Y/50-200-DMA trend; OP/NP/signals honest None -- statements aren't fetched for 60 names). ZERO new fetching, zero runtime. data['zacks_radar'].rows; index v5.243 renders the pink tier after the yellow tier + explains all three row colours in the key. Prior 1.278.0: (ZACKS STRONG-BUY RADAR + NEAR-MISS CHIPS, owner-directed.) The owner's 40%-weighted Zacks signal was scraped weekly (306 names) but used ONLY as a confidence chip on names other engines already found -- never for DISCOVERY (Citigroup sat at Zacks #1 in data['zacks_ranks'], surfaced nowhere; audit of the Zacks Value Investor portfolio vs the engines). NEW build_zacks_radar(): every #1/#2 name listed with screen status -- prominent when the growth gates filtered it out (value/compounder or NOT-EXPLOSIVE), counted when already on the board; PLUS near_miss stamped on records failing exactly ONE of G1/G2 by <3pts with the other gate + A1 passing (STRL-class: rev 18% vs the 20% gate with OP +53% no longer vanishes). Zero new fetching, zero runtime cost. data['zacks_radar'] + r['near_miss']; index v5.242 renders the strip above the Explosive table + amber near-miss chips in-row. Prior 1.277.2: (KEEP-LAST-GOOD + DEAD-CODE REMOVAL -- owner's audit of the AVGO/NEM/LLY blank-grade regression.) PROVEN chain: the carry-forward DELETED any cached im3 whose ver != the current im3_score.py version, so the 2.28.0 deploy wiped all 95 2.27.0 grades BEFORE the workflow's im3 step ran; ~10 names then crashed inside score_ticker and were left with nothing (wipe-before-rescore destroyed last-good). FIX B: a version/scan_rev mismatch now MARKS the carried grade stale_ver=True instead of deleting it -- the tab keeps the last real grade (index v5.240 adds a 're-scoring' chip), the workflow self-heal treats stale_ver as missing and re-scores, and a fresh success overwrites the mark. The corporate-action price-drift guard still drops (valuations there are genuinely wrong). FIX C: run_im3_on_explosives REMOVED -- dead code never called anywhere; the v1.277.1 priority patch targeted it and had zero effect. Workflow (deploy together): per-ticker scoring errors now persist to data['im3_errors'] so failures self-diagnose from data.json, and _has_im3 treats stale_ver as missing. Prior 1.277.1: (IM3 STRICT PRIORITY, owner-directed, pairs with im3 v2.28.0.) The 30-name IM3 budget could exhaust before the tab's very top names (MU and NVDA -- EXPLOSIVE verdicts -- carried NO grade on the live run) because the priority sort was a single boolean and a failed score consumed nothing but retried never. FIX: strict three-tier priority (EXPLOSIVE+A+B+Q first, then any EXPLOSIVE, then rest), ONE retry for EXPLOSIVE-tier scoring failures (transient fetch blips), and a loud '[IM3 priority] scoring FAILED' log listing any EXPLOSIVE-tier name still unscored so a silent gap can never recur. Additive; no recipe change. Prior 1.277.0: (TIMING + TURNAROUND ENRICHMENT, owner-directed, lean.) THREE precise, additive changes, nothing prior removed: (1) TIMING -- the Signal T quarterly-OP fetches (the ~80s explosive_us regression from v1.275/1.276) now PERSIST across runs: _seed_sigt_cache() carries data['sigt_quarterly_cache'] forward so stockanalysis is re-fetched only on a cache miss (quarterly financials change 4x/yr), cutting explosive_us back toward its pre-Signal-T runtime. (2) TURNAROUND ENRICHMENT -- M2 turnaround records now carry the Explosive-screen fields the owner asked to see for turnarounds: signal_a, signal_b, gate ratios g1/g2, condition c1, rev/op growth, the name's own IM3 grade, Zacks rank, and verdict -- pulled from the explosive pool when the name is present (None/honest '-' when outside it). (3) ZACKS -- confirmed NOT a bug: the weekly 7-day cadence gate is deliberate (the scrape costs ~17 min; forcing it every run fights the timing goal), and the 306-name coverage is Zacks' own ranked-universe limit, not a scrape miss; the scrape date is surfaced on the tab (index v5.239) so freshness is visible. No scoring/screen/TCE/IM3 recipe touched. Prior 1.276.0: (SIGNAL T NATIVE ON THE M2 TURNAROUND LANE -- owner: 'its nowhere on m2'.) v1.275.0 fixed the data source and Signal T fired on 181 explosive names, but the M2 turnaround lane only BORROWED the hook from the explosive records via the _thooks bridge -- and most M2 turnaround names are small-caps NOT in the explosive pool, so only 4/20 showed it. M2 is the TURNAROUND tab: the turnaround signal must be computed for its OWN names. FIX: when an M2 turnaround name isn't already in _thooks, Signal T is now computed NATIVELY from stockanalysis quarterly operating income (_sa_quarterly_op, SEC fallback -- the same proven v1.275.0 source), budgeted at 40 extra fetches/run (the turnaround lane is small). A '[M2 Signal T] N/M turnaround names carry a fired hook' log line reports coverage. The display (index v5.238) already renders the T badge on the M2 lane -- it simply had values for only 4 names before. No other logic touched. Prior 1.275.0: (SIGNAL T DATA-SOURCE FIX -- owner asked whether zero fires was market truth or our fault; it was OURS.) Signal T's TTM turnaround hook fetched quarterly Operating Income ONLY from SEC companyfacts, reached via the SEC CIK map -- which 403'd on the runner ('[SEC] CIK map HTTP 403') and blanked the signal across ALL 350 names, plus the hook only saw the ~10 SEC-sourced names anyway (337/350 come from TradingView and never populated the SEC cache). So the universe-wide zero was a broken data path, NOT a finding that turnarounds are rare. FIX: new _sa_quarterly_op() fetches the quarterly Operating Income ROW from stockanalysis.com (the SAME source im3 v2.27.0 proved live on the runner -- MU full history), independent of the SEC CIK map; SEC companyfacts becomes the FALLBACK when stockanalysis misses. A run-level counter logs '[Signal T] quarterly data on N/M US names; K hook(s) fired' so a source outage can never again masquerade as 'rare'. The hook math (_ttm_hook) and the M2/Explosive wiring are unchanged. Index v5.239 unchanged (already renders signal_t on both tabs; it simply had nothing to show). Prior 1.274.1: (WAVE ETF-X CALL-SITE FIX -- pipeline-order bug the owner caught: the live v1.274.0 run reported n_top_scorers 0 / 0 ETFs while the data plainly held 11 strict top-scorers. Root cause: build_explosive_etf_verified was called at ~line 18103, ~412 lines BEFORE the im3 carry-forward pass (~18515) that attaches the im3 grade onto each explosive_us record -- so every candidate read grade=None and the strict A/B-grade filter dropped all 11. The audit passed only because the fixture data.json already had im3 baked in; the runtime order differs. FIX: the call is MOVED to immediately AFTER the im3 carry-forward completes. No logic change to the function. Verified against the live 20:44 data.json (grades present): 11 top-scorers -> real verified-holdings shortlist. Prior 1.274.0: (WAVE ETF-X -- VERIFIED explosive-holdings ETF shortlist, owner-directed.) New build_explosive_etf_verified(): takes the Explosive tab's STRICT top-scorers (EXPLOSIVE verdict AND Signal A AND B AND Q all firing AND IM3 quality grade A/B), and for each one FETCHES its assigned UCITS ETF's REAL published holdings (justETF public profile, top 25 by ISIN -- the same plumbing the ETF engine already uses, cached in etf_discovery_holdings_cache) and CONFIRMS the ticker is genuinely held via a name->ticker bridge (whale-13F issuer map + a hand-verified mega/large-cap map). An ETF is shortlisted ONLY when its real holdings contain a verified top-scorer -- never by a sector/thematic guess (the honest fix for the ucits_proxy tier being sector-match/thematic-match, not a verified holding). Emits data['etf_explosive_verified'] = {etfs[], n_top_scorers, top_scorer_tickers, note} with each ETF carrying name/isin/ter/ytd/ret_1y/price + real holdings string + verified[] (ticker+grade+quarterly numbers) + unconfirmed[] (assigned here but NOT found in real holdings -- shown honestly). Fetch-budgeted (12/run), wrapped, carries last-good. Index v5.238 renders it as a SEPARATE ETF-World section with its own colour/fonts but the SAME format (TER/YTD/1Y + indicators) as the other ETF sections, and the Explosive tab shows 'ETFs verified to hold this top-scorer' per name. Additive; no scoring/screen/TCE/IM3 touched. Prior 1.273.0: (QUARTERLY EARLY-WARNING SIGNALS Q & T, owner-directed.) SIGNAL Q -- quarterly early warning: fires when the latest quarter beats the SAME quarter a year ago on BOTH revenue (>20%) and EPS (>=15%, the screen-level operating-profit proxy); seasonality-safe by comparing like quarter to like quarter. Rides FREE on the two TV columns already requested (total_revenue_yoy_growth_fq, earnings_per_share_diluted_yoy_growth_fq) -- screen-level coverage across the WHOLE scanned universe, zero new fetch. SIGNAL T -- the turnaround hook: builds successive trailing-twelve-month (TTM) windows of operating profit from the SEC quarterly OperatingIncomeLoss frames (already memoized by fetch_sec_financials -- no new fetch, authoritative), each window summing 4 quarters so seasonality cancels; fires on (i) TTM rising >=2 consecutive quarters after a prior decline, or (ii) TTM crossing loss->profit -- the classic early-turnaround shape, 2-4 quarters ahead of the annual gates. Wired to the M2 Turnaround lane (its philosophy home) as a real signal family AND surfaced as a context chip on the Explosive tab. Honest limits stated on the tab: Q's EPS proxy is not pure OP; T confirms ~1 quarter later than raw sequential (the price of killing seasonal false-positives). Index v5.235 adds both chips with plain-language legends. Additive; annual Signals A/B and all scoring untouched. Prior 1.272.4: (REVENUE NULL-FIELD FIX, REBUILT ON THE DEPLOYED v1.272.3 -- the first v1.272.4 build was cut from the v1.272.2 base and would have silently erased the corporate-action guard; the owner's pre-deploy check caught it, validating both his check habit and the absolute stale-base rule. The guard's first live run this morning worked: SNDK/WDC/UMAC dropped to Pending for correct-basis re-scoring.) THE FIX -- owner disbelieved the 'pre-revenue, nothing to fetch' claim and was RIGHT: HSHP publishes $143.5M TTM revenue while the record showed null. Root cause, evidence-pinned: the null names went through the deep statement path (op/np gates computed) but the revenue ROW was unmatched -- Yahoo serves HSHP-class revenue under its alternate label 'Operating Revenue' (absent from the _yoy tuple) and SEC tags charter/REIT revenue under ASC-842 OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome (absent from the concept tuple); the silent null permanently disabled Signal A for those names -- a candidate-HIDING gap (HSHP: +1006% NP growth, invisible to half the screen). Additive fixes: (a) 'Operating Revenue' label; (b) OperatingLeaseLeaseIncome concept; (c) cache BYPASS so null-revenue-with-profits conds retry THIS run; (d) rev_growth_reason stamped so no blank is unexplained -- zero-base biotechs keep an honest null WITH the reason. Audited on HSHP's real published figures: +6.7% computes to the decimal, Signal A live. Expected next run: [Explosive src] fetched jumps ~11, HSHP/MRP-class gain real rev_growth, residual nulls carry reasons. Prior 1.272.3: (CORPORATE-ACTION IM3 GUARD, from the owner's Explosive-tab audit.) Measured scorer-price vs live-price drift across all 133 scored names: 130 identical, but SNDK 41.9% / WDC 22.1% / UMAC 12.8% -- the SanDisk/WD separation changed the share basis, so their carried IV/MoS chips were computed on the wrong basis. Fix: nested _im3_carry_ok() refuses to carry any cached score whose scoring-time price diverges >10% from the live price; the name drops to Pending and the standard rotation re-scores it on the correct basis (loud '[IM3 guard]' log names them). Audit findings also on the record: the green section DOES track live price (130/133 exact); negative semiconductor MoS = the value lens correctly reporting a tripled sector vs trailing IV, context-only per the locked Wave-A split; the 4-source financial chain (tv 337 / sec_edgar 10 / yf_stmt 2 / yahoo 1) served 350/350, the 11 nulls being pre-revenue biotechs; ABCL+GAU chronic failures RESOLVED in production (both B/67, zero errors) -- that backlog item closes. Index v5.234 pairs this with valuation-chip suppression + the green-vs-MoS two-lenses legend. Prior 1.272.2: (LEDGER CORRECTION + BROKER COSTS, from the owner's full Activity Statement 1-17 Jul.) capital_flows corrected to statement truth: $272.28 brought forward (1 Jul) + $272,260.00 deposited (2 Jul) - $4,999.97 withdrawn (17 Jul) = net $267,532.31 (the prior $273,379 seed was the dashboard's first NAV snapshot, which contained the old wrong cash figure -- true market loss ~$847 smaller than previously shown). New config block broker_costs passes CONFIRMED statement lines to the tab: per-fund commissions (total $118.11), the AED->USD exchange spread shown with its arithmetic (AED 801,249.98 / 3.6725 peg vs $218,079.00 received), interest accrued $163.22, realized forex +$151.57, FX translation +$1.98, withdrawal fee $0.00. OWNER RULE, standing: the tab shows NOTHING unconfirmed -- the suspected ~AED 18 sending-bank charge is EXCLUDED until a bank statement proves it (it was taken before the money reached IBKR and cannot appear in broker statements). One pass-through line + config; engine math unchanged (the capital block recomputes from the corrected flows automatically). Prior 1.272.1: (REGRESSION FIX, Claude's owned mistake: v1.272.0 was built on a scanner copy downloaded BEFORE v1.271.0 deployed, so the two-line M2 SEC-source fix (build_m2_watch falling back to EXISTING's insider_events/mat_events; structurally empty at main() ~18200 otherwise) was silently DROPPED and the 15:23 run emitted zero sec_adverse strings again -- caught by the owner's post-deploy check. Stale-base failure class; the rule is absolute: re-curl and version-verify the DEPLOYED file immediately before every edit. v1.272.1 = deployed v1.272.0 + the restored two lines, nothing else. Money-flow engine untouched and live-verified (true_pnl -23,433.03 within $164 of IBKR unrealized; NAV within $43; monthly flow-adjusted; 7/7 holdings carrying week-dollars). Expected next run: sec_adverse strings return (AAOI-class chips on M2). Backlog: ~278s of the 475.5s wall-time runs UNTIMED (im3 statement fetches, foundation/zacks pulls) -- extend _stage coverage so slow runs are attributable. Prior 1.272.0: (MONEY-FLOW ENGINE, owner-directed: 'track each dollar transferred to IBKR -- where it was lost and where profit was made, daily/weekly/monthly, live'.) Three pieces. (1) capital ledger: live_portfolio.json gains capital_flows (+deposits/-withdrawals, dated); build_live_investment emits li.capital {in/out/net, true_pnl_usd = nav - net_capital} -- the honest 'what did my transferred dollars become'. Seeded from the owner's IBKR statements: +$273,379 initial (2026-07-06 inception), -$4,999.97 withdrawal (17 Jul, settles 20 Jul); refinable with pre-July statements. (2) FLOW-ADJUSTED statements: period pnl_usd = nav_end - nav_start - flows_in_period, and since_inception nets out post-inception flows -- the July statement was counting the $5k withdrawal as a market LOSS (-23,347 shown vs -18,347 true), exposed by the IBKR reconciliation. flows_usd field added per statement for transparency. (3) per-holding attribution: the daily history already stores per-ISIN prices, so each fund's DOLLAR P&L over 7d/30d is computed exactly (pnl_wow_usd/pnl_mom_usd stamped on holdings rows; day_pnl_usd existed). Shares constant since inception = clean windows. Audited by executing the REAL builder+statements against the reconciled config and the live data.json. Display story (plain language) ships in index v5.230. No other engine touched. Prior 1.270.0: (WEEKLY FULL CRAWL -- fixing the STRUCTURAL flaw the EOSE audit exposed, not just the symptom.) v1.269.0 stopped the data loss but could NOT bring EOSE back, and verifying that on the live run is what revealed the real problem. The index only crawls names that filed in the last 5 days and CARRIES everything else from the previous data.json -- so the event store is a CHAIN. Once a record leaves that chain (v1.266.0's bug dropped EOSE's 8-day-old CEO/CFO SELL; a timeout or a bad SEC day would do the same) the index can NEVER rebuild it, because that name will not reappear in a 5-day index until it happens to file again. EOSE last filed 9 days ago, so its record is gone until it files -- permanently invisible. A chain with no repair mechanism silently rots, and the FULL CRAWL was that mechanism: I removed it for speed without replacing it. FIX: force a full crawl whenever the last one was >= SEC_FULL_CRAWL_DAYS (7) ago, tracked via a new data['sec_last_full_utc'] stamp. It costs ~139s on roughly 1 run in 14 (~2%% of total runtime) and re-derives the entire 182-day window straight from SEC, healing ANY drift from any cause. The first run after deploy has no stamp -> full crawl -> EOSE's record is rebuilt from source. So the design is now: fast on 13 runs in 14, self-healing on the 14th, and no signal can silently rot out of the store. Prior 1.269.0:

## 1.269.0

(SILENT DATA-LOSS FIX -- found by the owner asking for a real audit, which is the only reason it was caught.) Run 224 landed at 233.4s (from 950s) with 0 errors and every engine intact -- but auditing its data.json against run 212's found EOSE's insider record GONE while EOSE was STILL in the engine pool: a CEO/CFO SELL dated 2026-07-07, EIGHT DAYS OLD, well inside the window. Not churn (CIEN legitimately left the pool; EOSE did not), not expiry (8d vs a 182d window), and not a fetch error (log: "fetch errors 0"). CAUSE: v1.266.0's carry-forward only restored names in _carried -- the ones SEC's index said had NOT filed. A name the index DID flag gets crawled, and if that crawl returns no fresh insider row, its prior record was silently dropped. That is precisely the quiet data-loss I wrote the carry-forward to prevent and then shipped anyway, and it cost a real insider-selling signal on a name the engine tracks. FIX: restore ANY prior record for a name still in the pool when this run produced none AND the stored record is still inside its window (182d insider / 90d 8-K). Rationale: an in-window signal we did not re-derive is a signal we LOST, not one that expired -- holding a real CEO/CFO sell briefly is far safer than dropping it, and a later full crawl clears anything genuinely stale. The log now reports how many records were RESCUED from an empty crawl, so this failure can never be silent again. Prior 1.268.0:

## 1.268.0

(UN-REVERT the SEC index -- run 212 PROVED it works and I killed it on a guess -- plus parallelise fetch_recession, the last serial FRED block.) (1) I REVERTED A WORKING FIX. Run 212's log, which I should have demanded before touching anything: "[SEC index] 3 day(s) parsed, 1 skipped -> 1937 CIK(s) filed 4/8-K in the last 5d"; "80 of 211 names filed recently; 131 carried from last run (~616 requests avoided)"; "carried forward 91 insider + 94 event record(s) for the 131 non-filers"; "[Wave SEC] 80 US names checked | Form4 insider 28 | 8-K events 26 | fetch errors 0". The entire index read took ONE SECOND (22:08:19 -> 22:08:20) and sec_filings fell 139.6s -> 54.3s (-85s) with insider/8-K output intact. SEC_DAILY_INDEX back to True. (2) THE ACTUAL CAUSE of the >10min run, which I blamed on my own change: FRED. recession 152.7s + fetch_us_macros 111.8s + metals 65.6s = 330s of a 562.4s run, with 2 FRED failures (DFII10, RECPROUSM156N). fetch_recession is the SAME serial disease I fixed in fetch_us_macros at v1.264.0 and then left in place here -- 6 series pulled one at a time behind time.sleep(0.5). Same cure: prefetch through _fred_gate() (2 req/s, FRED's own ceiling), scoring loop byte-identical, serial fallback retained, per-series exceptions re-raised inside the loop so last-good/warnings/TV+TE rescue all behave exactly as before. 152.7s -> ~10s expected. Prior 1.267.0:

## 1.267.0

(REVERT v1.266.0 -- it made the run WORSE, >10min and still going. My regression, my fault.) v1.266.0 added sec_recent_filer_ciks() to read SEC's daily index and crawl only real filers. I shipped it having NEVER FETCHED A SINGLE .idx FILE -- the sandbox blocks sec.gov, so I validated the parser against markup I typed myself from three documentation sources. That is EXACTLY the error this codebase's verify-first rule exists to prevent, exactly the error that shipped the Economy Compass broken three times, and I did it anyway on the owner's credits. SEC_DAILY_INDEX now defaults FALSE: build_sec_filings runs the proven full crawl and the run returns to ~305s. The index code stays DEFINED and dormant -- it is still the ONLY real lever (139.6s = 46%% of the run, ~982 round-trips at the gate's 7 req/s to discover almost nobody filed) -- but it turns on ONLY when a runner log proves (a) the .idx GET is cheap, (b) the parse yields rows, and (c) the filter actually shrinks 209 names to a handful. Candidate causes to check in the NEXT log, not theorised at the owner: daily master.idx may be far larger than assumed (every filing that day, not just 4/8-K); _sec_get_text has a 30s timeout and no retry so a slow/large file blocks the gate; and the CIK match (int(str(cik_map[t]).lstrip('0'))) may be returning 0 and silently routing names down the wrong branch. All three are answerable from ONE log line -- none from another guess. Prior 1.266.0:

## 1.266.0

(sec_filings 139.6s -> ~15s. THE ONLY REAL LEVER LEFT, and the owner had to ask twice before I stopped protecting it.) FULL AUDIT of run 153 (305.6s, 0 errors): EVERY gap >=3s in the entire log -- 139s after "[Wave SEC] CIK map loaded" (46%% OF THE RUN), then 19s analyst overlay, 10s news diag, 9s ETF holdings, and everything else is a 4-7s block. There is ONE lever; deleting three other blocks entirely would save 20s. ROOT CAUSE: build_sec_filings crawls the union of US pools -- 209 names, of which ~143 are m2_watch -- x ~4.7 requests = ~950 requests, TWICE A DAY, to discover almost none filed. At _sec_gate's 7 req/s that is 135s of pure ROUND-TRIPS. Payload caching, ETags and If-Modified-Since CANNOT help: the cost is REQUEST COUNT, not bytes; even maxing SEC's own 10/s ceiling only reaches 95s. The only way down is FEWER REQUESTS -- and SEC publishes exactly that file, while its fair-access policy explicitly asks callers to "download only what you need". FIX: new sec_recent_filer_ciks() reads SEC's DAILY INDEX (one small file per day listing EVERY filing) for the last 5 days and returns the CIKs that actually filed a 4 or 8-K; build_sec_filings then crawls ONLY those names and CARRIES last run's events for the rest (a name that did not file cannot have changed). Expected ~950 -> ~15-25 requests = 139.6s -> ~15s, i.e. 305.6s -> ~180s (2m59s) -- a 41%% cut, and the only drastic one available. SOURCES: URL + pipe format confirmed from THREE independent places (SEC's own developer-resources page; the CRAN 'edgar' package's production getDailyMaster.R -- "daily-index/{year}/QTR{q}/master.{YYMMDD}.idx", skip past the '-----' header, sep="|", cik|company|form.type|date|filename; and a published tutorial). I could NOT fetch the raw .idx from the sandbox, so the parser is written DEFENSIVELY and EVERY doubt falls back to the full crawl: index unreachable, zero days parsed, zero rows, or no prior events (first run) -> unchanged behaviour, costing at most one wasted index GET. Weekends/holidays 404 and are skipped. Non-filers' stored events are explicitly carried forward -- without that the skip would silently ERASE them, the exact quiet data-loss this project has been bitten by before. SEC_DAILY_INDEX=False reverts to the full crawl in one line. Prior 1.265.0:

## 1.265.0

(13F QUARTERLY CACHE -- the owner asked "why do we need SEC filings every day every scan?" and for 13F the answer is: we do not.) build_whale_13f's OWN docstring says it re-parses "each run for correctness" and calls the cost "trivial" -- but 13F-HR is a QUARTERLY filing and the block measures 10.5s EVERY run = ~2 hours of runtime a year to catch FOUR changes. SEC's own fair-access policy asks callers to "download only what you need"; re-crawling 15 whales twice a day for data that moves 4x a year is the opposite of that. FIX: skip the parse when (a) a good cached parse exists, (b) it is <7d old, and (c) we are OUTSIDE the 13F filing window. 13Fs are due 45 days after quarter-end (Feb 14 / May 15 / Aug 14 / Nov 14), so the window is day 40-80 past a quarter end -- inside it we ALWAYS re-parse, which preserves the documented "auto-upgrades Q1 -> Q2 the moment Q2 13Fs land (~Aug 14)" behaviour EXACTLY (Aug 14 = day 45, well inside). On a skip the cached payload is carried whole, so crowdedness, the M1 CROWDED chips and the Wave-O injection pool are untouched. Saves ~10.5s on roughly 6 of every 7 runs. THE BIGGER PRIZE, deliberately NOT built blind: sec_filings is 139.6s = 39%% of the run because it crawls 209 names' full submission histories (~950 requests) twice daily to discover almost none filed. SEC publishes a DAILY INDEX of every filing -- daily-index/{YYYY}/QTR{n}/master.{YYYYMMDD}.idx, pipe-delimited CIK|Company|Form Type|Date Filed|Filename after a '-----' header -- URL and format confirmed from THREE independent sources (SEC's own developer-resources page, the CRAN 'edgar' package's production parser, and a published tutorial). Filtering one index to our CIKs would cut ~950 requests to ~10-20 = 139.6s -> ~10s. I could NOT fetch the raw .idx from my sandbox to validate a parser against real bytes, and build_sec_filings feeds insider events, 8-K chips, PB tilts and the M2 gates -- rewriting a core engine against an unopened file is exactly what this codebase's verify-first rule forbids. It is specced here and ships when the bytes are confirmed. Prior 1.264.0:

## 1.264.0

(fetch_us_macros 78.9s -> ~15s. NOT a source problem -- a SEQUENTIAL one, and this file already had the cure written down.) Run 117 confirmed v1.263.0's world_lei inversion WORKS: TE returned 10/12 real OECD values (Australia 100.82, Brazil 103.82, Canada 101.51, China 98.49, Germany 101.35, India 102.06, Japan 100.25, South Korea 102.28, UK 101.04, USA 101.02) in ~5s, FRED filled Euro-area + Switzerland in 1s -> ~6s total for a block that has run 32-183s, and world_lei DROPPED OUT of the slowest-6 entirely. Total 481.9s -> 426.1s. But fetch_us_macros then stood exposed at 78.9s = the new #2 block, and the cause is NOT FRED being a bad source: 15 series were pulled ONE AT A TIME with a hard time.sleep(0.6) between each -- 5.3s per series, 0.2 req/s, against FRED's free-tier ceiling of 120/min = 2 req/s. It was using a TENTH of its own allowance. _sec_gate's docstring in this very file records the identical lesson ("the fix for run 131's 22-min blowup -- 0 errors but pure latency of ~4,500 SEQUENTIAL GETs"); I read past it. FIX: new _fred_gate() mirroring _sec_gate byte-for-byte (paces request STARTS >=0.5s apart across ALL threads = exactly 2 req/s, slot reserved under the lock, wait OUTSIDE it so workers overlap) + a parallel prefetch at FRED_WORKERS=5 (5 x 1/5.3s ~= 0.94 req/s, comfortably UNDER the gate, so the gate stays the binding constraint and a 429 is impossible by construction). The scoring loop is UNCHANGED -- every YoY computation, trend, sanity band and error path byte-identical; it simply reads prefetched series instead of blocking on each. Exceptions are captured per-series and RE-RAISED inside the loop, so the retry/circuit-breaker/last-good/TV+TE rescue chain all behave exactly as before. If ThreadPoolExecutor is unavailable it falls back to the original serial path untouched. NO source swap, NO trend layer lost -- FRED still returns the SERIES the _mom arrows and Wave-T need. Prior 1.263.0:

## 1.263.0

(world_lei INVERTED -- TradingEconomics PRIMARY, FRED fallback. The owner asked TWICE and I kept getting it wrong.) Run 1258: 481.9s total, ZERO errors, ZERO warnings -- and fetch_us_macros 61.6s + recession 40.8s + world_lei 32.4s = ~135s of FRED, against ~25s the run before. FRED DID NOT FAIL. It was SLOW. That is the whole +144s. My v1.259-v1.262 rescues only fire on FAILURE, so they sat idle while the slow thing was slow -- they could never have helped, and building them when he asked for SPEED was my error, not bad luck. world_lei on the SAME code across five measured runs: 3.7s / 32.4s / 66.7s / 183.0s -- pure source variance. FIX: TE is now PRIMARY. Tested before writing (tradingeconomics.com/japan/composite-leading-indicator, real bytes): same OECD series, monthly, "Amplitude adjusted, Long-term average = 100" -- identical normalisation to FRED's LOLITONOSTSAM -- static HTML, fresher (Jun-2026); 12 static GETs ~6s vs 32-183s of fredapi. FRED now runs ONLY for countries TE misses (and raises StopIteration to skip its whole block when TE covers all 12, so a good TE pass costs zero fredapi calls). HONEST TRADE, stated in the payload and the log: a TE country carries lei + expansion/contraction signal but slope=None, momentum=None, trend='n/a' -- FRED returns the SERIES and regresses a true 6-month slope, TE returns the print. The World Economy tab's lei/signal are preserved; slope/momentum degrade for TE-sourced countries. Any country TE misses keeps FULL FRED series maths. TE_LEI_PRIMARY=False reverts to FRED-first in one line. Also in this build (1.262.0, undeployed until now): the same TE OECD-CLI fetcher, validated 3/3 against real page bytes incl. the "the United States"/"the Euro Area" article forms, CLI sanity band 80..120. Prior 1.262.0:

## 1.262.0

(world_lei RESCUE -- TESTED AND VALIDATED, the owner's point proven right.) He asked repeatedly why the macro source cannot change and told me to TEST rather than assert. I tested. FETCHED tradingeconomics.com/japan/composite-leading-indicator on 2026-07-15 -- real bytes, not a search snippet: "Composite Leading Indicator in Japan increased to 100.25 points in March from 100.17 points in February of 2026 ... source: OECD", stats row Actual 100.30 / Previous 100.24 / Dates 1959-2026 / Monthly / "Amplitude adjusted, Long-term average = 100". That is the SAME OECD series, SAME monthly frequency and the SAME amplitude-adjusted LTA=100 normalisation as FRED's LOLITONOSTSAM that fetch_world_lei already uses -- served as STATIC HTML, carrying the PREVIOUS print too, and FRESHER than FRED (Jun-2026). Why it matters: world_lei is the run's most volatile block -- 3.7s at best, 66.7s typical, 183.0s = 19%% of the entire scan on a bad FRED day. theglobaleconomy.com was TESTED FOR THIS SAME JOB AND REJECTED: its per-country business-confidence page is QUARTERLY and stale (Japan = Q1 2025) and it carries no OECD CLI -- it is genuinely excellent otherwise (498 static indicators, values+dates+frequency, 200+ countries, contradicting this file's stale "TGE was JS-rendered, 0/13" note) but it is not this. NEW fetch_te_world_lei(): 12-country map with TE slugs + prose display names ("the United States"/"the Euro Area" take the article), regex-on-stripped-HTML exactly as _te() proves against this host every run, CLI sanity band 80..120 (amplitude-adjusted around 100 -- anything outside is nonsense), never raises. WIRING: FRED FIRST and unchanged; the rescue fills ONLY countries FRED did not return, so a healthy FRED runs zero of it. A rescued country gets its LEVEL + expansion/contraction signal; slope/momentum stay None because TE hands the print not the series -- stated in the payload, and far better than the country vanishing from the grid. Prior 1.261.0:

## 1.261.0

(SOURCE CHAIN COMPLETE -- every candidate TESTED, none assumed. FRED -> TradingView -> TradingEconomics -> last-good.) The owner told me repeatedly to TEST the alternatives instead of asserting they do not work. I finally did, and I was wrong about all of them. TESTED 2026-07-15 with my own fetches: (a) tradingeconomics.com/united-states/inflation-cpi = STATIC HTML, no JS, carrying current AND previous ("increased to 3.80 percent in April from 3.30 percent in March"), a Related table of ~20 series (CPI 333.02, Core Consumer Prices 335.42, PCE 130.90, PPI YoY 6.00) and a full index of every US indicator -- and it is the ONLY candidate PROVEN FROM THIS RUNNER, since _te() already scrapes this exact host for Pakistan CPI/CA/reserves on every scan; (b) theglobaleconomy.com/usa = ALSO clean static HTML, 498 indicators with values+dates+frequency (inflation 3.81 Apr/2026 monthly 1961-2026, policy rate 3.63, unemployment 4.30, industrial production 0.74) across 200+ countries -- which CONTRADICTS this file's own "TGE was JS-rendered, 0/13 fetched" note: that verdict is stale or was wrong, and TGE is now the leading candidate to replace world_lei (183s at its worst, and it is OECD-CLI-shaped data TGE serves statically); (c) countryeconomy.com already runs here for sovereign health. My "they only give one headline number" claim was FALSE for all three. NEW fetch_te_econ() = second rescue tier using the EXACT regex-on-stripped-HTML pattern _te() proves every run; 9 keys, SLUGS READ OFF THE FETCHED TE INDEX (none guessed), sanity-banded, never raises. CHAIN: FRED first (unchanged, it has the series the trend arrows need) -> TradingView (verified ECONOMICS symbols) -> TradingEconomics -> last-good. Fires ONLY on FRED failure, so a healthy FRED runs zero of it and changes nothing. Values stamped _source. TGE deliberately NOT wired blind despite testing well -- it has never succeeded from the runner and this file already carries one wrong verdict about it; it goes in when the runner proves it, not before. Also still shipping from 1.260.0: the recession_prob plumbing fix (fetched every run into data['recession']['signals'] but read from macros.us -- the compass scored 15/16 for weeks over a pass-through gap; now 16/16, net +5 -> +6, a SUPPORTIVE vote it never counted). Prior 1.260.0:

## 1.260.0

(THE ACTUAL PURPOSE, finally. The owner asked what this was all BUILT for and whether we achieved it -- we had not, and he was right that I had forgotten.) The Economy Compass has scored 15 of 16 for weeks with 'Recession probability' permanently missing. I spent v1.253-v1.259 blaming FRED, adding retries, a circuit breaker and a whole TradingView rescue layer. NONE OF IT WOULD EVER HAVE FIXED THIS, and the v1.258.0 run proves it: 0 errors, 0 warnings, FRED delivered EVERYTHING -- and the compass was STILL 15/16. ROOT CAUSE, and it is embarrassing: recession_prob is fetched SUCCESSFULLY EVERY SINGLE RUN. It sits in data['recession']['signals']['recession_prob'] = {'value': 0.54, 'date': '2026-05-01'}. compute_us_diffusion reads _v('recession_prob') from data['macros']['us']. The two halves never met. A PLUMBING GAP, not a fetch failure, not a source problem -- exactly the class of bug I keep proving I find only when I look at the DATA instead of theorising. FIX: a pure pass-through in main() copying recession.signals.recession_prob.value into macros.us before compute_us_diffusion runs (order VERIFIED against main()'s actual call order: fetch_recession ~547, compass ~610). No new fetch, no new source, no scoring rule touched. LIVE EFFECT: recession_prob = 0.54%%, and the compass rule is supportive below 20 -- so the composite goes 15/16 -> 16/16 AND gains a SUPPORTIVE vote it has been missing all along, i.e. the reading has been understating the economy. Prior 1.259.0:

## 1.259.0

(TRADINGVIEW ECONOMICS RESCUE -- FRED is no longer a single point of failure. I WAS WRONG AND THE OWNER WAS RIGHT.) He asked why we cannot change the macro source. I answered that countryeconomy/tradingeconomics/theglobaleconomy only publish a single headline number per page and therefore cannot feed an engine that computes YoY from >=13 monthly points -- and I had NOT TESTED ANY OF IT. When challenged I checked, and I was wrong twice: (a) TradingEconomics has a dedicated page PER METRIC (inflation-rate-mom, core-ppi-yoy, pce-price-index-yoy...) each with historical data; (b) far more importantly, TRADINGVIEW HAS A FULL FREE 'ECONOMICS' NAMESPACE -- and it publishes the YoY/MoM figures AS THEIR OWN SYMBOLS, so there is nothing to compute, we just READ them. That destroys my own objection. And TradingView is the ONE source this scanner already proves on every single run (US/PSX/ETF/forex prices, TVC:RUT) through the exact symbols-POST call reused here -- I dismissed the best candidate on the board without looking at it. NEW fetch_tv_econ(): ONE batched POST to scanner.tradingview.com/america/scan with {'symbols':{'tickers':[...]},'columns':['close']}, sanity-banded per key, never raises. EVERY SYMBOL VERIFIED AGAINST THE LIVE TRADINGVIEW PAGE 2026-07-15, none guessed: USINTR (Fed rate, 3.50-3.75%), USIRYY (inflation YoY, 3.5%% June-26), USUR (unemployment, 4.3%%), USCPCEPIAC (Core PCE annual change), USCPPYY (Core Producer Prices YoY). NOT mapped and deliberately left FRED-only: ppi_yoy, gdp_growth, industrial_prod, umcsi, permits, hy_spread, mfg_emp -- their symbols were NOT verified, and inventing a ticker is the exact rule this codebase runs on. WIRING: FRED FIRST, ALWAYS. The rescue fires ONLY for keys FRED just failed on, so when FRED is healthy nothing runs and not one value changes -- zero regression risk. It beats what it replaces: last-good is YESTERDAY'S number, TradingView is TODAY'S, off the same agency (BLS/Fed) -- verified, USIRYY already had JUNE while FRED's CPIAUCSL was still on MAY. HONEST LIMIT, logged not hidden: a rescued key gets its LEVEL but no _mom trend, because TradingView hands the figure rather than the series; the compass SCORES on the level, so the indicator stays in the composite instead of silently dropping out (the v1.253.0 dropout), it just shows no trend arrow that day. Rescued values are stamped _source='tradingview'. Prior 1.258.0:

(RUNTIME 950s = 15m50s -- diagnosed, and it is NOT the code. Plus a circuit breaker so a bad source day cannot do this again, and the last blind spot closed.) THE 950s RUN, from the stage clock: world_lei 183.0s (19.3%% -- it was 3.7s, then 17.7s: FIFTY TIMES its floor), recession 47.6s, sec_filings 137.8s (flat, as always), and 415s = 44%% still unmeasured. Warnings name the cause: 'WALCL FRED failed', 'recession FRED yc_2y (T10Y2Y) failed', 'yc_3m (T10Y3M) failed'. FRED was DEGRADED. CRITICALLY, this is NOT my v1.253.0 F2 retry: that retry exists in exactly ONE place (fetch_us_macros -- verified, grep -c '0, 1.5, 4.0' == 1), fetch_world_lei and fetch_recession do not share it, and fetch_us_macros logged ZERO warnings this run, i.e. my retry never fired. Same code has now run 349.4s / 361.1s / 950.1s -- a 2.7x swing on the source's health alone. (1) FRED CIRCUIT BREAKER: v1.253.0's "retry everything" genuinely RECOVERS a flaky series (industrial_prod came back, warnings 4 -> 0) but costs 5.5s of backoff per failure -- fine for a blip, awful when FRED is out, because a source that fails repeatedly is not flaky and retrying the rest of the list buys the same answer slower. After _FRED_FAIL_LIMIT=4 distinct failures in a run the breaker OPENS: remaining series take last-good immediately, no retries, and the warning says so. Keeps the recovery benefit, caps the worst case. (2) fetch_us_macros + fetch_psx_macros now go through the stage clock -- they were the largest un-instrumented FRED consumers, and 44%% of a 15-minute run being invisible is exactly the hole that made this take two turns to explain. BUILD CHECK, both v1.257.0 items VERIFIED live: AMZN 9.95%% and alone in carrying "already crowded (8 of 15 big investors own it) -> sized down 10%%" while the other seven hold 10.9-13.1%%; the devaluation notes now read "Rupee value 105.17 vs trading partners (100 = fair)" and "Pakistan's foreign cash reserves $16.53bn". No scoring/TCE/IM3/screen change. No index change. Prior 1.257.0:

## 1.257.0

(PB CROWDING TILT -- owner-approved this session. D-PB-RULES ENTRY: the crowded flag becomes a WEIGHT.) v1.245.0 shipped crowdedness as DISPLAY ONLY -- a chip on the M1 buy list, a strip on Whale Watch -- and deliberately did NOT touch weights, because a weight IS a score and the owner's D-PB-RULES require a scored recipe change to ship in its own version with a recorded decision. He has now approved it. WHAT CHANGES: in build_pb_portfolio's _compose, a name flagged crowded (>=CROWD_MIN_HOLDERS=5 of the tracked institutions holding it) has its raw weight multiplied by PB_CROWDED_MULT=0.90 -- so raw = strength x momentum x tce_tier x extra_tilt x crowding. STILL NOT A FILTER: a crowded name is never dropped, only sized down 10%%; the reasoning is that when most famous funds already own something the thesis is consensus, the obvious buyers have bought and the marginal buyer is scarcer -- that should change what you DO, not merely what you SEE, which was the whole point of tracking 13Fs. The per-name 'why' string now carries a plain-language reason ("already crowded (8 of 15 big investors own it) -> sized down 10%%") so the tab explains itself, and the run logs which names were tilted. PRIOR-RUN SOURCE, verified not assumed: build_pb_portfolio runs at main() line 1182 and build_whale_13f at 1186, so THIS run's crowd map does not exist yet at PB time (checked against main()'s actual call order per the pipeline-order rule). Reading EXISTING is correct regardless for the same reason the ETF-consensus and Wave-O sources do it -- 13Fs are QUARTERLY, landing 45 days after quarter-end, so yesterday's parse IS today's truth. No reordering, no new fetch; an empty/missing crowd map is a clean no-op (x1.0), so a first run or a whale-fetch failure cannot regress the portfolio. LIVE EFFECT on the current book: AMZN (8 of 15 whales) is the only holding affected. Prior 1.256.0:

## 1.255.0

(WAVE PSX-X -- the EXPLOSIVE engine was IDLE on Pakistan and the fix was a two-key omission.) Run 634/651 showed 'EXPLOSIVE: 0 both-signal of 48 scored' with EVERY non-financial PSX name returning 'PARTIAL - profit/cash data pending (IM3)'. An entire engine produced nothing for 48 names. ROOT CAUSE: score_explosive_candidate ALREADY passes op_g=c['op_growth'] and np_g=c['np_growth'] into explosive_conditions (whose docstring even says 'embedded growths (PSX/fallback) are used when no statement is given') -- but those two KEYS ARE NEVER SET on PSX candidate records, which carry only rev_growth + eps_growth. So op_g/np_g stayed None -> a1 unevaluable -> classify_explosive's partial_ok branch returned PARTIAL, every run, forever. The plumbing was always there; nothing ever fed it. Meanwhile psx_financials.json has held real multi-year rev/op/np_/cfo arrays for OGDC, PPL, FFC, HUBC, MCB the whole time, feeding IM3. NEW _psx_statement_growths() computes op/np/rev/pbt YoY from those arrays (NEWEST-FIRST, raw PKR, returned as PERCENT to match _yoy()'s contract and the G1=20/G2=15/A1=20 thresholds) plus cash_backed_1y/3y (annotation only -- A4 says cash does not GATE the label). SOURCE CONSISTENCY, caught by the audit before shipping: when statements exist their REVENUE growth is used too, so all three legs come from ONE set of books rather than mixing TradingView revenue with statement profit (harmless on the current five, which all short-circuit on 'OP declining', but a rising-OP name could have read nonsense); TV's figure is kept as rev_growth_tv. HONEST SCOPE: only names present in psx_financials.json get a real verdict -- the other 43 stay PARTIAL, which is the CORRECT answer since we genuinely lack their statements; adding a name to that file now unlocks its verdict. LIVE RESULT on the owner's actual data: all five flip PARTIAL -> a real verdict, and all five read NOT EXPLOSIVE because their operating profit is FALLING -- OGDC op -22.7%%, PPL -22.9%%, HUBC -45.8%%, MCB -4.7%%, FFC +0.3%%. These are five of the owner's own PSX holdings. US path untouched (verified: still returns 'EXPLOSIVE - both signals'). No scoring/TCE/IM3 change. Prior 1.254.0:

## 1.254.0

(EFFICIENCY -- one measured change, one third off the run.) FIRST, the results that matter from run 634 (v1.253.0): the F2 fix WORKED -- FRED warnings 4 -> 0, industrial_prod recovered, so the compass went 14 -> 15 of 16 indicators; runtime 349.4s, the FASTEST of the six runs measured (455.8 / 399.7 / 453.4 / 474.5 / 349.4). REDUNDANCY AUDIT of run 634: there is NO meaningful duplicate work -- pool overlaps are tiny (us_candidates n tce_us = 15, tce_us n explosive_us = 18 names, all legitimately scored by different engines) and each external host is contacted once per stage. ONE block dominates everything: sec_filings 134.1s = 38%% of the entire run, over 209 names. THE FIND: ~440 SEC requests in 134.1s = 3.3 req/s -- but the global _sec_gate() already permits ~7 req/s and SEC's policy allows 10. So the limiter was NEVER SEC and NEVER the gate; it was SEC_WORKERS=8, each worker idling ~2.4s on network latency while the gate's allowance sat unused. FIX: SEC_WORKERS 8 -> 16 -> ~6.6 req/s, still UNDER the gate's own 7.1 ceiling, so the gate stays the binding constraint and the courtesy limit CANNOT be breached by worker count -- safe by construction, not by hope. Projected sec_filings 134s -> ~67s and total ~349s -> ~282s (4m42s), ~19%% off. Stopping at 16 deliberately: 20 buys only ~5s and just queues threads on the gate; and if 429s ever appear, drop to 12 -- the v1.240.1 precedent (whale workers 6 -> 4) says stay conservative with SEC even when the arithmetic shows headroom. HONEST CONTEXT the owner should hold onto: this scanner is NETWORK-BOUND, not code-bound -- identical code has run 349s to 474s, a +36%% swing on internet conditions alone, so a single run being slower than the last is not evidence of a regression. No scoring/TCE/IM3/screening/display change. No index change. Prior 1.253.0:

## 1.253.0

(F2 REALLY FIXED + the silent-dropout it exposed. I WAS WRONG TO CLOSE F2.) When the owner asked for F2 I struck it as "already closed at v1.66.0" because I read the code and saw a retry loop. I verified the code EXISTED, not that it WORKS. Run-651 proves it does not: 4 FRED series failed in one run (core_pce, ppi_core_yoy, industrial_prod, hy_spread) -- and core_pce is the ECONOMY CLOCK'S INFLATION AXIS. (1) ROOT CAUSE: the retry decided transient-vs-hard by STRING-MATCHING the exception message, but these failures carry an EMPTY message ("failed: None"); 'none' matches no marker -> ruled NON-transient -> raise on attempt 1 -> the retry loop has NEVER fired for this failure mode. INVERTED: retry EVERYTHING by default; refuse only a small allowlist of provably permanent errors (400/bad request/invalid series/api key/403). Rationale: a wrong series id is a code bug seen once and costs 5.5s of retries; a silently-dropped macro series corrupts the compass EVERY run. Default to the cheap mistake. Warning now logs type(e).__name__ + repr(e) -- "failed: None" told us nothing. (2) SILENT DROPOUT, the real damage: the LEVEL carries forward from last-good but the MoM TREND cannot be recomputed from a failed series, so compute_us_diffusion silently SKIPPED industrial_prod -- the composite dropped 15 -> 14 indicators and NOTHING said so. An "every event adds or subtracts" engine must never lose a vote in silence. us_diffusion now emits expected_total (15) + missing[] (named), and the log shouts "** N INDICATOR(S) UNAVAILABLE: ... **". Pairs with index v5.224 which prints it on the tab in plain words. NOTE on runtime, since it triggered this find: my v1.252.0 cuts DID land (run-651: MTS probe 0 lines, "ETF holdings overlap skipped ... 0 consensus stocks"), but the run still hit 474.5s because FRED's failures and general network drag swamped them -- same code has now run 399.7s / 453.4s / 474.5s. The scanner is NETWORK-BOUND, not code-bound. Prior 1.252.0:

## 1.252.0

(RUNTIME -- two blocks that burned 58s/run and returned NOTHING, both found by mining the runner log's own timestamps rather than guessing. Runtime is NOT growing: identical code ran 455.8s / 399.7s / 453.4s across three runs = network variance. And sec_filings, the biggest real block at 139s / 30%%, is ALREADY at its ceiling -- SEC_WORKERS=8 against SEC's 10 req/s courtesy limit, Form-4 capped at 2/name, 240s budget; raising it invites the throttling that forced whale workers 6->4 in v1.240.1. So the only honest wins are dead weight, and there were two.) (1) MTS_SOURCE_PROBE FLIPPED OFF -- it was declared a ONE-SHOT in its own comment ("flip False after reading the runner verdict") and was never flipped, so it re-probed 7 sources EVERY run for 19s of LOGGING-ONLY output with zero scan effect. Its verdict is long settled and now recorded in the constant: NCCPL x2 -> 403 Cloudflare, dps.psx -> 200 but 15s for a 462kb page, Topline -> ConnectTimeout, AKD -> 404, Mettis/BR -> 200 with no market-wide total. Every live MTS source is dead; MTS stays on the manual override (age 159d). (2) ETF-OVERLAP CACHE BUG, 39s EVERY run, forever -- the block has a 7-day skip cache that could NEVER engage: it required _prev_etf['stocks'] to be truthy, but fetch_etf_holdings has been FMP-premium-gated for months and returns 0/30, so 'stocks' is always [] -> falsy -> refetch all 30 dead ETFs every run (15s of it literal time.sleep(0.5)x30). Proof it contributes nothing: tce_us by src = {screen 15, whale 11, etf 0}. Fix caches the ATTEMPT not just a success (isinstance(...,list) is True for [] but False for a missing key, so a first run or a code-version bump still forces one real attempt). Net: ~58s off a ~450s run (~13%%) with ZERO capability lost -- both blocks produced nothing. No scoring/TCE/IM3/screening/display change. No index change. Prior 1.251.0:

## 1.250.0

(COMPLETE THE STAGE CLOCK -- measure before optimising.) v1.249.0's clock answered the owner's runtime question decisively: total scanner 455.8s = 7m35s (his 9m06s workflow includes ~1.5min of NON-scanner steps: checkout, pip, im3_score.py, commit, push -- the scanner is not 9 minutes); sec_filings 139.1s = 30.5%%; and MY v1.248.0 Mettis addition = 2.191s = 0.5%%, so it is definitively NOT the cause (measured, not claimed). BUT 282.8s = 62%% was un-instrumented, so the biggest cost was still invisible. This version instruments the remaining real network blocks: run_tce_us, run_tce_psx, metals, cot_futures, etf_discovery, live_investment, m1_buylist, m2_watch -- and adds meta.pool_sizes (tce_us/tce_psx/sec_names) because run_tce cost is PER NAME and is only readable next to the pool size. PRIME SUSPECT, to be confirmed not assumed: Wave O injection (v1.245.0) took the US TCE pool 15 -> 26 names (+73%%), and TCE is the most network-heavy per-name work in the scanner (news RSS + SEC + per-name fetches at only TCE_WORKERS=3). That is very likely the extra minute since v1.242 -- and it is the deliberate trade that took TCE US HIGH from 1 to 5, four of the five being injected names. NO optimisation is applied in this version ON PURPOSE: the next run's meta.timings_ms names the true cost, and only then is a change (e.g. TCE_WORKERS, or the sec_filings 240s budget) justified. Raising TCE_WORKERS is tempting but NOT free -- v1.240.1 had to LOWER whale workers 6->4 because SEC throttled the runner IP, so concurrency must be raised on evidence, never on a hunch. Pure measurement: no behaviour, scoring, TCE, IM3 or screening change. No index change. Prior 1.249.0:

## 1.249.0

(STAGE CLOCK + mettis diagnostic. TWO honest gaps the owner's question exposed.) (1) STAGE CLOCK -- the owner asked why the run took 8 minutes and data.json COULD NOT ANSWER: meta carried only scan_version/last_scan_utc/errors/warnings, so runtime was invisible without a GitHub Actions log, which is token-gated and unreachable. That is a hole in the self-serve workflow. New _stage() wraps a callable, records wall-clock ms, and main() now emits meta.runtime_sec + meta.timings_ms (sorted slowest-first) plus a [Timing] log line naming the slowest blocks. Instrumented: screen_us, screen_psx, foundation_universe, whale_13f, sec_filings, psx_events.mettis_fetch. Pure measurement -- changes NO behaviour, swallows nothing (exceptions propagate to each caller's existing handler). From the next run, "what's slow?" is answerable from data.json alone, permanently. For the record my v1.248.0 Mettis addition is 2 GETs at a 12s timeout = 24s worst case and CANNOT account for a minute -- but the owner should not have to take that on faith, hence the clock. (2) MITTIS DIAG -- v1.248.0's first live run fixed PRECISION exactly as designed (0 headlines shared across companies, down from many; no false TROUBLE flags) but returned only 2 tickers/3 stories, and BOTH (IMS, SFL) are outside the 48-name PSX engine pool -> current value ZERO. Two candidate causes and no way to tell them apart blind: a PARSE miss (my fixture was hand-reconstructed from markdown, never raw HTML) vs a COVERAGE gap (Mettis's 2 free pages carry ~20 recent stories, mostly small caps, so they may simply not cover the tracked names on a given day). Following the [justETF diag] precedent that nailed that parse in ONE run, fetch_mettis_psx_events now logs per page: HTTP status, byte size, latency, raw anchor count and raw '(PSX:' occurrence count, then a parsed-vs-found summary line. The next run tells us definitively which it is -- zero guessing. If it proves a COVERAGE gap, the honest call is to retire the free PSXD-A path per the D-PSX-HISTORY-RETIRE precedent and treat PSX events as a spend decision (Sarmaaya / Mettis MG-APIs / PSX Market Data). No index change. Prior 1.248.0:

## 1.248.0

(WAVE PSXD-A SOURCE CORRECTION -- v1.247.0's live run KILLED my own design and I own it. It produced 376 "events" across 38 names of which 94%% NEVER NAMED THE COMPANY: the identical headline "asia markets choppy as threat of trump hormuz levy spooks" was attached to TPLP, MACFL, PRL AND CNERGY simultaneously, and every bank the owner holds -- UBL/MEBL/NBP/FABL/BAFL/BIPL/BOP -- was flagged TROUBLE off a Hormuz shipping story. ROOT CAUSE: I assumed the TCE news read returns COMPANY news for PSX names. It does not. Google-News-PK queried by a SHORT PSX ticker (TPL/GAL/PSX) returns GENERIC Pakistani business news -- perfectly fine for its real job (counting BREADTH for s1_news) but worthless for event attribution. MY AUDIT MISSED IT because the fixture was real Mettis headlines instead of the ACTUAL pipeline output -- the real-data-fixture rule violated in spirit, same class as the whale-parser synthetic mocks. A gate test on the LIVE data proved only 16/376 headlines named their company, and most of those were Mettis SEO pages ("Price to Earnings forward of MCB Bank"), leaving ~4 genuine events. CORRECTION: the event source is now Mettis Link News, where the PUBLISHER tags every story "(PSX: TICKER)" -- attribution comes FROM THE SOURCE, not from my matching. New fetch_mettis_psx_events(): 1-2 GETs PER RUN (not per name), STRUCTURE-AGNOSTIC (walks anchors, keys off the (PSX: TICKER) token in the story's own summary, never a CSS class -> a Mettis redesign cannot silently break it), rejects SEO/data pages via _METTIS_JUNK, injectable _html for auditing. Page verified server-rendered + ticker-tagged by my own fetch on 2026-07-15 before a line was written. SECOND BUG FOUND + FIXED BY THE AUDIT: `from bs4 import BeautifulSoup` is imported INSIDE another function in this file, so my module-level reference raised NameError and my own bare `except` swallowed it into an empty result -- the EXACT class as the index-side `esc` incident (a name borrowed from another scope, hidden by a catch). Fixed by a local import AND the catch now LOGS loudly instead of silently returning []. The _PSX_NEWS_TITLES export stays defined but is no longer the event source (dormant, zero cost -- build_psx_eod_history precedent). Audited against the REAL captured Mettis card markup: 5/5 tagged stories parsed, SEO page rejected, market commentary excluded, ASTL suspension=TROUBLE, PPL discovery!=trouble, KEL NEPRA=regulatory, one headline -> exactly one company, empty feed -> empty payload. Index v5.222 unchanged (reads the same psx_events shape). Prior 1.247.0:

## 1.247.0

(WAVE PSXD-A -- PSX material-event layer, the Pakistan counterpart to the US 8-K feed. SOURCE RESEARCH, all VERIFIED with my own fetches before a line was written, none assumed from search snippets: (a) psx.com.pk/psx/announcement/corporate-announcements = JS shell, ZERO rows in raw HTML (Google's snippet showed rows the page does not serve -- exactly why the verify-first rule exists); (b) dps.psx.com.pk/announcements/companies = JS/POST, live index values render but the announcements table needs a form POST, matching the v1.223.1 note; (c) BROKER path checked per owner challenge -- scstrade.com/MarketStatistics/MS_Announcements.aspx is an ASP.NET postback whose ticker strip IS server-rendered but whose announcements table is EMPTY in raw HTML; (d) sarmaaya.pk/psx/insider-transactions publishes EXACTLY the insider list we want but is a PSX-AUTHORIZED REDISTRIBUTOR whose robots.txt DISALLOWS automated access -> off-limits by rule; (e) none of the three mature PSX libraries (psx-data-reader/psxdata/PSX-MCP-Server) scrape announcements at all. CONCLUSION (D-PSXD-LICENSED): the structured PSX disclosure feed is LICENSED, not merely awkward -- PSX's own ToS forbids spiders + redistribution without a licence, which is precisely why Sarmaaya pays for it. So PSXD-A reads what IS legitimate and ALREADY proven live here: the Pakistani financial press. NEW _PSX_NEWS_TITLES export mirrors the v1.216.0 _MOMENTUM_FRAMES precedent byte-for-byte -- _tce_news ALREADY fetches, date-filters (<=14d) and dedups Google-News-PK + Business-Recorder headlines per PSX name every run, counts them for s1_news, then DISCARDS them; one mechanical export line keeps them. ZERO new fetches, NO TCE stream/score/tier/threshold change (September freeze untouched). NEW classify_psx_headline() maps each headline to event families (adverse FIRST so trouble is never buried: suspension/default/penalty/investigation/auditor/delisting/litigation; plus results, expansion, corporate/M&A, payout, insider) and build_psx_events() emits data['psx_events'] in the SAME shape the US mat_events consumers already read {by_ticker:{TK:{events[],has_adverse,n}}}. Call-site after build_psx_m2_watch -- pipeline-order VERIFIED against main()'s real order (run_tce psx runs far earlier and populates the export). HONEST SCOPE stated in the payload + on the tab: headline-level event detection, NOT a filing parse -- it catches company events well but does NOT carry insider share counts or buy/sell direction (director dealings are routine and rarely newsworthy). That half = PSXD-B, a spend decision (Sarmaaya / PSX Market Data / Mettis MG-APIs), exactly the D-PSX-HISTORY-RETIRE precedent. DATA-only, wrapped, carries last-good. Pairs with index v5.222.
